## 1 GENERAL DISCLOSURES [ESRS 2]

### 1.1 General basis for preparation of sustainability statements [BP-1]

The Sustainability Statement of MOTOR OIL Group (hereinafter "the Group" or "MOTOR OIL Group") for the reporting period 1 January to 31 December 2024 has been prepared in accordance with the European Sustainability Reporting Standards (ESRS). It serves both as a compliance reference under applicable regulations and as a demonstration of the Group's ongoing commitment to transparency, accountability, and responsible business practices. This report provides stakeholders with insights into environmental, social and governance (ESG) actions, initiatives and impacts and covers the value chain of the Group, including the impacts, risks and opportunities (IROs) identified in the upstream and downstream supply chain as well as the Group's own operations.

The Sustainability Statement has been prepared on a consolidated basis, aligned with the financial statements to ensure consistency in the reporting scope. It covers all MOTOR OIL Group entities, including subsidiaries and Joint Operations (JOs), as well as Associates and Joint Ventures (JVs) and reflects their material Impacts, Risks and Opportunities across all business operations. The reporting period follows the financial year, ensuring comparability between financial and sustainability information and is referenced to appropriate financial indicators when needed.

In continuation of previous years, the Group has conducted a double materiality assessment that incorporates impacts, risks and opportunities across the entirety of the value chain, while considering key areas such as emissions, resource utilization, labor conditions, as well as business conduct. The Group's policies, actions, targets and metrics reflect the efforts to reduce environmental impacts, enhance resource efficiency and promote ethical labor practices. It has to be noted, that all available value chain data are integrated into sustainability metrics to ensure coverage (in cases where this was not possible, it is duly noted).

Finally, the narrative of the Sustainability Statement is structured to follow the Group's core business functions as outlined in the table, ensuring a comprehensive presentation of sustainability impacts, risks, opportunities and actions.

MOTOR OIL Group has not omitted any sustainability-related disclosures due to intellectual property, ongoing negotiations, or innovation protection. All relevant information has been fully incorporated into this report.

### 1.2 Disclosures in relation to specific circumstances [BP-2]

MOTOR OIL Group has defined time horizons for impacts, risks and opportunities in alignment with the ESRS framework. These time horizons are categorized as short-term (current to one year), medium-term (two to five years) and long-term (beyond five years).

Sustainability metrics incorporate data from both upstream and downstream value chain activities. Where direct data is not available, industry benchmarks and proxy data are used, with reasonable accuracy. In any case, the Group is committed to continuously enhance data quality through engagement with suppliers and business partners to improve transparency across the value chain, a fact that will be certainly improving during the next reporting period. For the time being, several metrics have been based on larger- or lower-scale estimations. These include:. Scope 3 emissions. Non-employees (contractors)

Furthermore, there are metrics that include estimates of upstream and/or downstream value chain data using indirect sources, mostly referring to Scope 3 emissions and non-employees. The references for all estimates are documented in detail in the corresponding sections below. For every methodology used and the assumptions made, these issues are addressed clearly in the corresponding chapters. This approach is chosen, as such estimates are limited to the energy mix of Greece, the emission factors used for the emission calculations and to certain internationally recognized net calorific values for energy conversions. The Group is committed to limit the use of the assumptions made for the following years, depending on the availability of data and the progress of scientific research in the petroleum sector regarding its value chain.

This Statement has been prepared in accordance with Laws 4403/2016 and 4548/2018, which transpose EU Directive 2013/34/EC, as amended, into Greek legislation. It also complies with the provisions of Law 5164/2024 (Government Gazette A 202/12.12.2024), which incorporated Directive (EU) 2022/2464 of the European Parliament and Council into national law. These legal frameworks establish the obligation for large undertakings classified as public interest entities, as defined in Annex A of the Directive, to prepare both a Management Report and a Non-Financial Statement. Additionally, this Statement includes the disclosures required under the Corporate Sustainability Reporting Directive (CSRD), as implemented through the European Sustainability Reporting Standards (ESRS), which introduce enhanced sustainability reporting requirements for companies operating within the EU. The statement references additional frameworks and commitments, including the Global Reporting Initiative (GRI), IFRS S1,S2 and the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs). Additionally, the report references the EU Taxonomy Regulation, ensuring alignment with the European framework for classifying environmentally sustainable economic activities.

#### 1.3 Governance

The following information can be found in the Corporate Governance Statement 2024:

1\. A detailed description of the role of the BoD and its committees (Audit Committee, Remuneration Committee and Nominations Committee).

2\. The composition and diversity of the members of the undertaking's administrative, management and supervisory bodies including experience relevant to the sectors, products and geographic locations as well as business conduct matters of the undertaking.

3\. A description of how the administrative, management and supervisory bodies determine whether appropriate skills and expertise are available or will be developed to oversee sustainability matters, including (a) the sustainability-related expertise that the bodies, as a whole, either directly possess or can leverage, for example through access to experts or training; (b) how those skills and expertise relate to the undertaking's material impacts, risks and opportunities.

### 1.3.1 The role of the Administrative, Management and Supervisory Bodies [GOV-1]

#### Supervisory Bodies

The Board of Directors holds ultimate responsibility for defining and overseeing the Group's business and sustainability strategy, ensuring that ESG considerations are fully embedded into long-term value creation, risk management and strategic planning, in alignment with corporate objectives and evolving regulatory requirements. It also regularly evaluates the opportunities and risks associated with the defined strategy, as well as the effectiveness of the measures implemented to address them. Several Board members bring relevant sustainability experience in areas such as energy transition, risks and regulatory compliance and five of them serve on the Group's Sustainability Committee actively contributing to the integration of sustainability into both strategic direction and operational oversight. To support this role, all Board members have completed targeted sustainability training designed to address emerging regulatory frameworks and the environmental, social and governance impacts, risks and opportunities facing the organization in the global energy sector. There is no representation of employees and other workers in the Board of Directors.

The Audit Committee is responsible for overseeing the review and approval of non-financial disclosures, ensuring the integrity, reliability and regulatory compliance of sustainability-related reporting. Its members possess expertise in both financial and non-financial matters, including sustainability and have received targeted training on ESG impacts, risks, opportunities and the audit process for sustainability reporting.

The Nomination Committee is responsible for ensuring that both the management and supervisory bodies possess the appropriate sustainability-related expertise, including business conduct matters, to support the Group's strategic direction. The Chairman of the Nomination Committee also serves as a member of the Sustainability Committee, reinforcing the alignment between governance oversight and sustainability leadership.

The Remuneration Committee is responsible for integrating sustainability-related performance indicators - recommended by the Sustainability Committee - into executive and management incentive schemes, reinforcing the link between ESG outcomes and performance-based rewards. The Chair of the Remuneration Committee also serves as a member of the Sustainability Committee, ensuring close coordination between sustainability objectives and compensation frameworks.

The Sustainability Committee was established in June 2021 and consists of board members. The committee is responsible for designing and overseeing the Group's Sustainability Policy and Strategy. It defines strategic objectives, reviews material ESG topics (Including Impacts, Risks, Opportunities) and performance and approves annual and long-term ESG-related targets. The committee works in close cooperation with the Sustainability Division. The composition of the committee is the following:

Chair: Petros T. Tzannetakis (Executive Board member)

Members:

John N. Kosmadakis (Executive Board member)

Michael-Matheos J. Stiakakis (Executive Board member)

Rania N-P Ekaterinari (Non-Executive / Independent Board member)

Niki D. Stoufi (Non-Executive Board member)

Convener: Helena Athoussaki - Chief Sustainability Officer

The Sustainabitliy Committee meets at least once a year and additionally, as needed, when deemed necessary by the Committee's Chairman. Minutes of the meetings are kept, which are approved by the members of the Committee and then signed by the Chairman and the members.

In 2024, the Committee convened in September 2024 where all members of the committee participated.

Key duties:. Oversees the Group's Sustainability Strategy, ensuring alignment with the Group's vision, values and overall business strategy.

· Supervises sustainability due diligence processes to identify, assess and address material ESG risks and impacts. Monitors the content and completeness of sustainability reports and all external ESG-related communications, ensuring transparency and consistency.. Reviews and approves management proposals related to the content of the sustainability strategy and submits recommendations to the Board of Directors for final approval.. Monitors the effectiveness of the Group's ESG and sustainability-related risk management framework, ensuring appropriate governance and oversight.. Sets ESG priorities and both short- and long-term targets, oversees the implementation of sustainability initiatives and monitors the progress and performance of these actions.. Oversees and evaluates the Group's overall contribution to society, ensuring that ESG efforts generate positive social impact.. Approves sustainability and ESG-related policies and procedures, ensuring alignment with international standards and regulatory requirements.. Endorses the Double Materiality Assessment process, ensuring that the Group's ESG strategy addresses the most relevant and significant issues.. Reviews and approves proposals regarding the Group's commitments to climate change and decarbonization, ensuring alignment with global frameworks.. Collaborates with other Board committees on cross-cutting ESG topics, promoting a coordinated and integrated governance approach.. Works closely with the Sustainability Division and the internal ESG Working Group, providing guidance and oversight in the implementation of sustainability goals.. Evaluates the ESG performance of both the Committee and the executive management team, ensuring that ESG responsibilities are executed effectively and contribute to the Group's overall strategic objectives.

The sustainability competencies of the members of the Sustainability Committee are derived from a combination of factors, including their experience in risk management, knowledge of ESG-related regulatory frameworks and deep expertise in the energy sector. These are complemented by strong strategic thinking capabilities and the ability to evaluate business plans and long-term scenarios. In addition, all members have received targeted training on sustainability regulations, emerging ESG trends and sector-specific developments, ensuring they remain up to date and well-equipped to guide the Group's sustainability agenda.

#### Management Body

The Chief Executive Officer (CEO) and Deputy CEOs are the highest-ranking executives within the Group, responsible for overall strategic leadership and corporate oversight. They provide direction and ensure alignment of business objectives with the Group's long-term vision and stakeholder expectations.

The General Managers, reporting to the CEO and Deputy CEOs are responsible for executing the Group's strategy across operations and business activities, including the integration of sustainability.

<!-- Report Page 66 -->
#### Administrative bodies

The Chief Sustainability Officer (CSO) leads the overall sustainability agenda and reports directly to executive leadership and the Sustainability Committee. The CSO works in close coordination with the Sustainability Committee to ensure ESG strategy aligns with the Group's overall business objectives and regulatory expectations. This role ensures that the group's ESG strategy is effectively translated into practice across all levels.

The CSO advises executive leadership on sustainability-related risks and opportunities, guiding the Group's strategic response and transformation. Acting as a convenor, the CSO provides strategic and operational support to the Sustainability Committee - coordinating inputs, facilitating discussions and ensuring that decisions are based on accurate ESG data, regulatory insights and cross-functional collaboration.

The Sustainability Division serves as the operational core of MOTOR OIL Group's sustainability strategy, responsible for translating strategic objectives into measurable action. Comprising experienced professionals in sustainability reporting, control management, ESG compliance and environmental management, the division plays a central role in embedding sustainability into the Group's operations and strategic planning. It is tasked with the implementation and oversight of environmental, social and governance (ESG) initiatives, ensuring that all activities are aligned with international sustainability standards and the Group's corporate goals. The division also monitors ESG performance, supports crossfunctional collaboration and drives continuous improvement across all business units.

Key duties:

· Managing and enhancing the Group's ESG reporting systems, consolidating ESG data across all pillars and ensuring the accuracy, compliance and integrity of the annual ESG report. It oversees the preparation, review and alignment of all sustainability disclosures with applicable standards, regulations and internal audit requirements.

· Developing the Group-level double materiality assessment and supports the development of short- and long-term sustainability strategies.. Monitors key ESG performance indicators, including carbon emissions, waste management and external ESG ratings. It tracks the financial impacts of sustainability-related risks and opportunities, performs gap analyses and communicates findings to support continuous improvement across the Group.. Ensures Group-wide compliance with ESG regulations, policies and frameworks, including climaterelated obligations. It oversees the implementation of relevant ESG standards and stays informed on emerging legislation and industry best practices to maintain regulatory readiness.. Serves as the primary liaison for all ESG-related communications, engaging with internal and external stakeholders such as auditors, regulators, investors and rating agencies. It leads the implementation of the Group-wide stakeholder engagement plan and manages responses to ESG questionnaires and information requests.. Ensures the accuracy, consistency and credibility of all ESG disclosures, acting as the gatekeeper for data shared externally. It serves as the primary contact for internal and external audits on sustainability matters and maintains strong internal controls to support reliable ESG reporting and assurance processes.. Identifies sustainability and technical risks, proposing mitigation measures and supporting technical assessments related to Health, Safety and Environment activities. It contributes to the development and validation of energy transition scenarios, decarbonization roadmaps and action plans, while also reviewing technical inputs from external consultants to ensure alignment with ESG standards and regulatory frameworks.

· Collaborates closely with Subject Matter Experts and Internal Audit to ensure timely and accurate ESG data collection and reporting. It supports the design, execution and monitoring of ESG-related projects and works across functions and sites to ensure consistent implementation of sustainability initiatives throughout the Group.

The Internal Control System (ICS) at MOTOR OIL Group consists of three core units: the Compliance Unit, the Risk Management Unit (RMU) and the Internal Audit Department. Together, these functions ensure effective corporate governance, regulatory compliance and risk mitigation across the organization.

The Compliance Unit ensures adherence to applicable laws, regulations and internal policies. It supports prudent decision-making by advising management and the Board, through the Audit Committee, on the Group's compliance status and emerging regulatory risks. The Unit ensures alignment with applicable laws and regulations, provides ongoing guidance to staff through internal policies and codes of conduct and promotes a strong ethical and compliance culture. It also ensures the Group adapts effectively to legal changes, helping to mitigate regulatory risk and identify potential business opportunities within the evolving compliance landscape. The Compliance Unit works closely with the Sustainability Unit to ensure alignment between regulatory compliance and the Group's ESG commitments.. The Risk Management Unit (RMU) identifies, assesses and manages risks across the Group. RMU maintains an effective risk management framework to support the ongoing development, implementation and enhancement of risk practices at the levels of systems, processes and organizational structure. The Unit maintains the corporate risk register - which includes ESG - related risks - and submits it along with an annual risk report to the Board of Directors highlighting significant risks and areas requiring action. Functionally reporting to the Board and administratively to the Deputy CEO, the RMU ensures that all business activities operate within established risk tolerance levels, covering risks across processes, systems and the Group as a whole. The Risk Management Unit works closely with the Sustainability Division to ensure that the ESG risk register is regularly updated, relevant departments have identified and assessed sustainability-related risks and their financial impacts and that appropriate mitigation measures are being developed and implemented across the Group.. The Internal Audit Division conducts independent audits to ensure that the Company and its subsidiaries operate in accordance with internal rules, the Articles of Association, Board decisions and approved governance manuals. It verifies compliance with job descriptions, procedures, the Manual of Authorities (MoA) and the organizational structure. The Internal Audit Department works in close coordination with the Sustainability Division to ensure the accuracy, integrity and reliability of ESG disclosures and sustainability-related processes. It provides independent assurance that sustainability controls are effectively implemented, ESG reporting aligns with internal policies and regulatory requirements and risks - particularly those related to sustainability - are appropriately monitored and managed.

Working Groups coordinated by the Sustainability division play a pivotal role in addressing IROs and implementing ESG related policies, plan, actions and targets. Each Working Group is cross-functional and may include representatives from various departments and subsidiaries, ensuring actively contribution to the Group's sustainability performance, transition planning and long-term ESG objectives.

Sustainability Representatives are appointed within each Group subsidiary or business unit to act as key intermediaries between local operations and the central Sustainability Division. This role will be enhanced to lead the implementation of ESG initiatives at the local level, collect and report data using the Group's ESG tool and ensure alignment with the Group's overall sustainability strategy and compliance obligations. They will manage local ESG activities, monitor performance and report progress to the central team.

<!-- Report Page 68 -->
##### 1.3.2 Information provided to and Sustainability Matters addressed by the undertakings' Administrative, Management and Supervisory Bodies [GOV-2]

The organizational structure of the Group reflects a clear allocation of sustainability responsibilities across supervisory, management and administrative bodies, ensuring that material impacts, risks and opportunities (IROs) are systematically addressed throughout strategic planning, risk management and operational execution.

#### Supervisory Body - Board of Directors (BoD)

The Board of Directors and the relevant committees holds ultimate accountability for overseeing the Group's overall business and sustainability strategy as described in the previous section. They are informed minimum twice a year through dedicated sessions and updates from the Chief Sustainability Office, Risk Management Unit and executive leadership about (a) Material sustainability-related risks and opportunities; (b) Progress in due diligence implementation across environmental, social and governance (ESG) areas; (c) Key decisions related to long-term strategy, major transactions and investment plans that may have significant sustainability implications. The BoD evaluates these elements in the context of their potential financial and non-financial impacts, considering trade-offs and alignment with the Group's risk appetite and long-term objectives. Final approval for major transactions and capital-intensive projects - particularly those related to energy transition - is granted by the BoD after reviewing detailed analysis and risk assessments.

The Sustainability Committee supports the BoD as described in the previous section. The Committee is briefed at least once a year by the Chief Sustainability Officer (CSO), who serves as a convenor and strategic advisor, ensuring alignment across functions and subsidiaries. It also facilitates crossdepartmental coordination on climate risks, ESG data and due diligence.

#### Management Body - Senior Management

General Managers are responsible for executing the Group's strategy across operations and for all business activities. Under the leadership of the CEO and Deputy CEOs, all divisions are actively involved in implementing the Group's sustainability agenda. Together with the Sustainability Division they contribute to identifying and assessing climate-related Impacts, Risks and Opportunities (IROs), ensuring that material sustainability factors are integrated into business planning.

The Sustainability Division coordinates sustainability policies and action plans, working closely with the management body. Additionally, all divisions support the definition and monitoring of sustainability KPIs which are reviewed by the General Manager to ensure alignment with the Group's strategic goals and continuous improvement.

#### Administrative Bodies - Sustainability Division, Risk Management Unit, Compliance and Internal Audit

The Sustainability Division operationalizes the Group's sustainability strategy by coordinating data collection, monitoring KPIs, supporting materiality assessments and leading stakeholder engagement. It collaborates with all the Groups' divisions forming working groups across ESG pillars to ensure timely and accurate ESG integration into the business model and long-term plans.. The Risk Management Unit (RMU) works closely with the Sustainability Division to ensure ESG risks are embedded in the corporate risk register. RMU ensures that business units identify financial implications of ESG risks and develop mitigation strategies, which are reported to the BoD.. The Compliance Unit works in close coordination with the Sustainability Division to monitor legal and regulatory developments in ESG, promote internal awareness and align policies with evolving standards such as the CSRD, ESRS and EU Taxonomy.. The Internal Audit Division provides independent assurance over ESG reporting, compliance processes and the integrity of sustainability-related controls and disclosures.

Material sustainability matters are embedded in the Group's key strategic and investment processes:. M&A transactions and Major Project Investments follow a structured governance pathway, from origination through approval by Senior Management and the BoD. ESG risks and due diligence are integrated throughout, including scenario planning and valuation assessments.

Long-Term Strategic Planning involves scenario-based forecasting and integration of sustainability performance targets. The Corporate Development and Strategy teams work with Planning & Budgeting (P&B) and Sustainability to ensure that ESG priorities, including decarbonization targets and climate transition planning, are reflected in the Group's forecasts.

During the reporting period, the administrative, management and supervisory bodies of the Group actively addressed a range of material sustainability-related impacts, risks and opportunities (IROs) identified through the Group's double materiality assessment process.

In total, the Group evaluated 38 sustainability-related impacts, of which 27 were assessed as material (13 Environmental, 9 Social and 3 Governance), along with 2 entity-specific impacts. Additionally, 41 sustainability-related risks and opportunities were identified, with 28 considered material (14 Environmental, 9 Social and 3 Governance).

Given the high volume and diversity of topics, these material impacts, risks and opportunities have been further grouped and categorized for clarity and will be presented in detail in the relevant section of this report.

These material IROs were reviewed and addressed as follows:. Supervisory Body (Board of Directors & Sustainability Committee): Oversaw the strategic implications of the identified IROs, particularly those with potential financial consequences. The BoD reviewed risks that could impact financial performance, cash flows and access to capital and ensured appropriate mitigation strategies were incorporated into business planning and investment decisions.. Management Body (Senior Management): Evaluated and monitored the integration of these material IROs into the Group's sustainability strategy and targets. The senior management also guided the implementation of ESG risk mitigation actions, including scenario planning for climate-related impacts and opportunities.. Administrative Bodies (Sustainability Division, Risk Management Unit, Compliance Unit): Collected, assessed and tracked performance related to material IROs. These teams played a key role in operationalizing the due diligence process, populating the ESG tool with relevant data, updating the ESG risk register and advising on both negative financial impacts and positive opportunities.

<!-- Report Page 70 -->
##### 1.3.3 Integration of Sustainability-related Performance in Incentive Schemes [GOV-3]

The Remuneration Committee of MOTOR OIL Group, composed of three non-Executive Directors (two of whom are independent), oversees the Group's remuneration policy, which links executive compensation to both financial and non-financial performance criteria, including sustainability factors such as climate change and Health & Safety.

The policy includes a combination of fixed and variable components. Fixed remuneration is granted to all Directors, while variable pay - linked to Group performance and subject to General Assembly approval - includes bonuses and long-term share-based incentives such as stock awards and options. Independent Non-Executive Directors receive only fixed fees to preserve impartiality.

To ensure fairness and alignment with strategic goals, remuneration is benchmarked against industry standards and tied to indicators including profitability, growth and corporate social responsibility. The Remuneration Committee regularly reviews and updates the policy, which is valid for up to four years, to maintain competitiveness and integrity.

##### 1.3.4 Integration of Sustainability-Related Performance in Incentive Schemes [E1.GOV-3]

The Group's Remuneration Committee, in collaboration with the Board of Directors and the Sustainability Committee, is integrating ESG objectives - such as GHG emission reduction - into the performance evaluation framework. The Sustainability Committee, supported by the Sustainability division, oversees the development and updating of relevant sustainability metrics.

Sustainability-related KPIs are progressively being embedded in remuneration policies, particularly within annual bonus schemes, aligning executive incentives with individual and corporate ESG goals. While the methodology is still being finalized, future disclosures will clarify the proportion of variable pay tied to these targets.

Long-term incentives, including stock awards and options, form part of the variable remuneration for executives (and, selectively, non-executives). These rewards are linked to both financial and non-financial performance, reinforcing the connection between sustainability outcomes and total compensation.

##### 1.3.5 Statement on Due Diligence [GOV-4]

MOTOR OIL Group is committed to embedding sustainability into its governance structure, ensuring responsible business practices that align with regulatory frameworks such as the Corporate Sustainability Reporting Directive (CSRD) and European Sustainability Reporting Standards (ESRS). The due diligence process at MOTOR OIL Group is designed to identify, prevent, mitigate and account for actual and potential negative impacts on the environment and society.

MOTOR OIL Group has established a Sustainability Committee (SC), responsible for overseeing sustainability due diligence. This committee ensures:. Integration of sustainability into corporate strategy and risk management processes.

· Establish clear roles and responsibilities for sustainability performance.. Compliance with international frameworks, including the UN Guiding Principles on Business and Human Rights and the OECD Guidelines for Multinational Enterprises.

The Group's Chief Sustainability Officer (CSO) collaborates with operational departments to execute sustainability policies and align them with business objectives.

MOTOR OIL Group integrates sustainability due diligence into all aspects of its operations, value chain and investment decisions. This includes:. Conducting risk and opportunity assessments to understand how sustainability factors impact financial performance.. Embedding Environmental, Social and Governance (ESG) criteria into procurement, supplier contracts and partnership agreements.

· Implementing business ethics policies to detect and address unethical practices early.

· Embedding Due Diligence into Internal Policies.

To ensure sustainability due diligence is part of daily operations, MOTOR OIL Group has developed and implements the following policies:

. Whistleblowing and Ethics Policy - Encouraging employees and stakeholders to report unethical practices without fear of retaliation.

. Anti-Corruption and Compliance Policy - Preventing bribery and corruption in business transactions.

Climate Adaptation and Carbon Management Policy - Mitigating environmental risks through investments in renewable energy and emissions reduction.

· Supplier Code of Conduct - Setting social, environmental and human rights expectations for supply chain partners.

MOTOR OIL Group also leverages its due diligence framework to enhance stakeholder trust and long-term corporate resilience by aligning with double materiality principles (assessing both impact materiality and financial materiality).

### 1.3.6 Risk Management and Internal Controls over Sustainability Reporting [GOV-5]

MOTOR OIL Group's risk management and internal control processes related to sustainability reporting, form an integral part of the Group's broader risk management framework. In compliance with Law 4706/2020, the Group has established a comprehensive Internal Control System (ICS), encompassing the Internal Audit Unit (IAU), the Risk Management Unit (RMU) and the Regulatory Compliance Unit. The ICS framework includes key components such as Risk Management, Control Environment, Control Mechanisms and Risk Controls, Information and Communication Systems and Continuous Monitoring.

The Risk Management Unit (RMU) is involved in identifying, assessing and managing the risks faced by the Group. Its primary objective is to establish an effective risk management framework, ensuring the development, implementation and continuous enhancement of risk management practices at the process, system and corporate levels.

The RMU ensures that the risks undertaken by the Group's business units align with the risk appetite and tolerance levels set by senior management. The Unit reports functionally to the Board of Directors (BoD) and administratively to the Deputy CEO for matters related to daily operations.

The Board of Directors holds ultimate responsibility for ensuring the adequate and effective operation of the Group's Internal Control System, which is designed to identify, assess and manage material risks associated with the Group's business activities and operations.

The Group deploys the Three Lines Model, a governance structure that enhances risk management and accountability:. First Line of Defense: Business units that directly manage risks arising from their activities.. Second Line of Defense: Units such as the Risk Management Unit (RMU) and the Compliance Unit, which provide oversight, guidance and supervision to the first line.. Third Line of Defense: The Internal Audit Unit (IAU), which operates independently and evaluates the effectiveness of the risk management framework.

The RMU maintains regular communication with the Internal Audit Unit (IAU) to ensure alignment, enhance risk oversight and avoid duplication of efforts.

The risk assessment approach consists of three key phases:

Risk Identification & Evaluation - Identification of uncertainties, potential threats and opportunities, culminating in a comprehensive Risk Register.

Risk Response - Development of response plans, detailing existing controls and outlining additional measures to mitigate risks.

Risk Monitoring - Continuous oversight and assessment of risk management effectiveness.

Risk assessment is conducted at two levels: Inherent Risk Level (before controls) and Residual Risk Level (after controls). The Group categorizes and monitors identified risks in the Risk Register, structured according to the Group Risk Taxonomy, which includes the following risks:

#### Group Risk Taxonomy

Upon completing the Risk Identification and Assessment process, Risk Response Plans are developed for key risks, outlining existing controls and planning for enhanced risk mitigation measures. These plans are prepared by the relevant Business Units and evaluated by the Risk Management Unit (RMU).

Operational Units (First Line of Defense) continuously monitor risks within their areas of responsibility and submit periodic reports to RMU. RMU aggregates and analyzes reported data, conducts independent assessments of risk management procedures and ensures alignment between risk exposure and risk appetite set by the Board of Directors.

The Second Line of Defense Units (including RMU) further oversee the Risk Management System and conduct independent audits of First Line operations to ensure compliance and effectiveness.

Risk prioritization is based on quantified risk scoring, considering both the potential impact and likelihood of risks. Risks are systematically ranked and addressed based on their significance to the Group's critical business objectives, always in alignment with the Group's risk appetite and tolerance levels set by the Board of Directors.

By embedding strong risk governance, proactive monitoring and robust controls, MOTOR OIL Group strengthens its resilience, enhances decision-making and ensures long-term sustainable growth.

Regarding the internal control processes specifically related to sustainability reporting, including the double materiality assessment, the Sustainability division's ESG Controller has identified the relevant risks and control procedures applied for mitigating those risks.

The risk identification and assessment approach for sustainability reporting risks is directly associated with the overall framework applied by RMU, to ensure a consistent and comprehensive approach to risk management.

The risks and internal control procedures identified by the Sustainability division are incorporated into MOTOR OIL Group's ICS through their correlation with RMU's Risk Register to ensure its completeness and reliability.

Further, the Internal Audit Division executes independent assurance audit reviews upon the procedures followed by the Sustainability Division on a yearly basis, to ensure the adequacy and effectiveness of the internal controls mitigating the risks of the sustainability reporting process.

The findings from the risk assessment process, including elements related to sustainability reporting, are integrated into relevant internal processes through the development of risk management plans. These plans may involve acceptance, transfer, or reinforcement of existing controls to further reduce or mitigate risks. These plans are communicated to specific units as necessary.

Additionally, the findings from the risk assessment process, including sustainability reporting elements, are reported to the administrative, management and supervisory bodies through two channels:

Reporting obligations of the Risk Management Unit: The RMU submits an annual Risk Management Report to the Board of Directors, highlighting significant risks and areas requiring attention. The full risk and controls register contains information on risks faced at a Group level, incorporating data received from MOTOR OIL, subsidiaries and managed affiliates.

Reporting obligations of the Sustainability division: As mentioned earlier, the Group has established a Sustainability Committee responsible for approving and overseeing the Group's Sustainable Development Policy and Strategy. This includes Environmental, Social and Governance aspects that may impact overall strategy, investment plans, risk management, sales performance, mergers and acquisitions (M&A), or other major capital expenditures. The committee monitors performance against set targets and decides on corrective actions related to existing opportunities or risks.

## 1.4 Strategy

<!-- Report Page 76 -->
### 1.4.1 Strategy, Business Model and Value Chain [SBM-1]

MOTOR OIL Group is a diversified energy group comprising over 188 companies with a combined turnover of €12.2 billion, a workforce of more than 3,332 employees (incl. Associate companies) and a network of over 1,500 fuels stations across Greece, Cyprus and the Western Balkans. The Group's principal activities include oil refining and marketing of petroleum products both domestically and internationally, power generation across conventional and renewable energy sources and a diversified portfolio of circular activities.

#### Vision

To evolve into a leading Multi-Energy Group in Southeastern Europe, driving sustainable growth and innovation across the energy spectrum.

#### Mission

To operate efficiently, responsibly and profitably in the oil, gas and selected sectors, while actively investing in alternative energy sources-addressing evolving customer needs and contributing to the region's energy security and transition.

## Core Principles

MOTOR OIL Group has embarked on an ambitious energy transition program which will transform the Group into a sustainable, diverse, multi-energy leader in the region. The goal is to create services and solutions for customers and long-term value for the shareholders by investing diligently in the energy transition, while reinforcing its presence across traditional energy markets. Group's strategic pillars recognize the need for a reliable, sustainable and affordable energy mix; therefore, in 2022 the Group embarked on a €4.0 billion investment program across those pillars by 2030, directing more than €2.5bn towards low carbon energy projects and €1.5bn towards resilience, energy security and the enhancement of capabilities in conventional energy products.

MOTOR OIL Group ensures that the Group constantly evolves in order, to be in a position, to supply Fuels, Electricity and Recycled Resources to its customers at an affordable cost and on a dependable basis, positioning itself so it can best capture future commercial opportunities while achieving its energy transition targets.

## Fuels

MOTOR OIL Group believes that the energy mix of the future will include both fossil fuels as well as renewable and alternative fuels. It consistently invests in the production and distribution of these important products for consumers. Additionally, the Group continuously evolves its retail network, offering high quality products and decarbonization solutions, as well as a convenience proposition beyond the forecourt.

MOTOR OIL Group produce and supply its customers with:. Conventional fuels such as gasoline and diesel, which continue to serve critical transport and industrial sectors;

· Sustainable fuels, including advanced biofuels and e-fuels, supporting decarbonization in hard-toabate sectors like aviation and shipping;. Petrochemicals, which remain vital for industrial and consumer products, and are increasingly integrated with circular practices.

### 3 Electrification

MOTOR OIL Group expects that the world will require significantly more electricity in the future and will be prepared to address this demand, offering power from both flexible conventional and renewable power sources. Concurrently, the Group will be offering sustainable electricity and gas products as well as e-mobility services to the customers through the retail platforms.

MOTOR OIL Group offers:

· Renewable and thermal power generation, contributing to reliable and affordable energy supply;. Power and gas supply & trading, a fast-growing area leveraging our presence in electricity and natural gas markets;

· Energy storage and e-mobility, supporting grid stability and electric vehicle infrastructure;

MOTOR OIL Group believes that in circularity lies a very efficient and clean solutions for truly sustainable growth. The Group invests in developing an integrated value chain starting at the feedstock level across product streams generating significant value for years to come.

The activities of MOTOR OIL Group in this domain include:. Environment and waste management, ensuring responsible treatment and recovery of recyclable materials, as well as the production of renewable energy from waste;

· Lubricants regeneration, reducing waste and extending the lifecycle of valuable materials;. High-energy value feedstock collection, supporting the production of low-carbon fuels and contributing to the Group's decarbonization roadmap.

The total number of employees is 3,332 employees including employees from Associate companies (63 employees in Greece). These employees are allocated in Greece (3.246), Cyprus (21), Romania (8), Bulgaria (9), Serbia (7), Croatia (39), Skopje (1) and America (1). The total number of employees, excluding those from Associate companies, is 3,269, as stated in 'Note 1: General Information' of the Financial Statement. Additionally, the Group has established itself as a key player in renewable power generation, operating one of the largest portfolios in the region in terms of installed capacity. In line with its commitment to sustainability and innovation, the Group has expanded into the circular economy and alternative fuels sector, making strategic acquisitions in biofuels and waste management.

The Group is committed to:. Unwavering focus on strong execution targeting operational excellence, improved profitability under all commodity environments and disciplined value-accretive investments.. Development of platforms of sustainable growth across the energy spectrum, including in areas of renewable energy production and environmental services.. Delivering value to its shareholders, by maintaining strong cash flow generation and prudent management of the Group's financial resources.

#### 1.4.2 Group Structure

Please refer to the 'Director's Report', specifically Section 5 titled 'GROUP STRUCTURE - SIGNIFICANT SUBSIDIARIES & AFFILIATED COMPANIES'.

##### Value Chain

MOTOR OIL Group provides a wide range of products and services. These include refined fuels such as gasoline, diesel and jet fuel; lubricants; energy products such as natural gas and electricity, focusing on renewable energy; environmental services such as waste management and recycling initiatives; and technical and maintenance services including engineering, safety and environmental compliance services.

The Group serves a wide range of markets and customer groups. In the automotive sector, it serves individual consumers, automotive service providers and fleet operators. The industrial sector includes manufacturing and power generation companies. The commercial sector includes retail stations, wholesalers and distributors. The residential sector serves households with natural gas and electricity. In addition, the environmental sector includes municipalities, local governments and environmental project developers.

The business value chain of MOTOR OIL Group and its subsidiaries comprises several key stages, from the procurement of raw materials to the final consumption and disposal of products. The value chain is divided into upstream, own operations/midstream and downstream activities.

##### Upstream

The raw material supply phase involves the procurement of crude oil, feedstocks, catalysts, additives and other chemicals necessary for refining processes. Key resources such as electricity, water and natural gas are utilized in the production process. Logistics activities include the transportation of raw materials and their storage.

##### Midstream - Own Operations

The refining process, which is central to the value chain, involves converting crude oil into various products, ranging from liquified gases, naphtha, gasoline, diesel, jet fuel, lubricants etc. This process necessitates advanced refining technology and strict adherence to safety and environmental compliance standards. Continuous maintenance and engineering services are crucial to ensure the smooth operation of refining process.

Once refined, the products are distributed to wholesalers and retailers. This phase involves coordinating logistics for transporting refined products to various distribution points. Finally, sales and retailing activities involve selling refined products directly to consumers, as well as providing fuel cards and retail outlets.

##### Downstream

The consumption and use phase involve the utilization of the final products by consumers, vehicles and industries. This phase includes monitoring the performance of the products and gathering feedback to improve future offerings. End-of-life management encompasses the handling of used oil and lubricants, with recycling initiatives implemented to ensure environmentally responsible disposal.



<!-- Report Page 82 -->
##### Main Features of Upstream and Downstream Value Chain

The upstream value chain of MOTOR OIL Group encompasses the supply of raw materials, resource utilization and logistics. Key components include:. Sourcing crude oil, feedstock and essential chemicals required for refining processes. Optimizing the use of electricity, water and natural gas to enhance efficiency in production operations

· Managing the transportation and storage of raw materials and refined products to ensure a seamless supply chain

MOTOR OIL Group's downstream value chain focuses on the distribution, consumption and end-oflife management of products. Key components include establishing efficient logistics and distribution networks to deliver refined products to wholesalers and retailers, ensuring timely availability, facilitating the use of products by various customer segments, incorporating monitoring and feedback mechanisms to continuously improve product offerings and implementing recycling initiatives and environmentally responsible disposal methods for used products, such as oil and lubricants, to minimize environmental impact.

### Sustainability Strategy

At MOTOR OIL Group, sustainability is not a standalone initiative - it is fully embedded within the corporate strategy, guiding investment decisions, operational priorities and long-term value creation across its entire business ecosystem. The Group is committed to promoting sustainable development, fully aligned with key global initiatives such as the European Green Deal, the Paris Agreement and the United Nations Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs). Group's sustainability strategy aims to provide investors with a comprehensive understanding of the risks and opportunities associated with their investments.

MOTOR OIL Group Sustainability Policy serves as the foundation of its sustainability strategy - providing the principles, commitments and governance framework as follows:. Decarbonization & Energy Transition: MOTOR OIL Group is implementing an accelerated energy transition plan to transform the Group into a diversified regional energy leader. This includes investments in innovative sustainable technologies, pioneering the adoption of renewable energy sources and enhancing energy efficiency for both Group's operations and customers.. Stakeholder Engagement & Materiality: The Group prioritizes engagement with stakeholders to understand their needs, concerns and expectations. By identifying and addressing key ESG issues related to MOTOR OIL Group activities, it strives for continuous improvement in Group's sustainability performance.. ESG Impact Assessment & Taxonomy Alignment: The Group conducts rigorous ESG impact assessments to ensure compliance with industry standards and regulatory requirements. MOTOR OIL Group operations align with recognized taxonomies, promoting transparency and comparability in the sustainability performance.

· ESG Policies & Procedures: MOTOR OIL Group has implemented robust ESG policies and procedures that guide Group's operations and decision-making processes. These policies emphasize ethical conduct, environmental stewardship and social responsibility.. Corporate Governance: The Group is committed to strong corporate governance practices that promote transparency, accountability and integrity throughout Group's business operations.. Monitoring Goals & Performance Indicators: MOTOR OIL Group sets clear and ambitious targets, supported by key performance indicators (KPIs) to track Group progress in sustainability. Through regular assessments and reporting, the Group identifies areas for improvement and drive meaningful change.

· Climate & ESG Risk Management: The Group proactively manages ESG and climate-related risks, safeguarding its business and stakeholders. This involves integrating risk management practices into the strategic planning and implementing mitigation measures to address potential impacts.

· Transparency, Reporting & Communication: The Group is committed to transparent communication of its initiatives, challenges and achievements through comprehensive reporting mechanisms, ensuring that stakeholders remain well-informed.

MOTOR OIL Group has launched one of the most ambitious Energy Transition initiatives in Southeastern Europe, aiming to evolve into a Sustainable, Diversified, Multi-Energy leader in the region. Group's strategy focuses on delivering innovative energy solutions that meet the evolving needs of the customers while generating long-term value for the shareholders. By strategically investing in the energy transition, the Group is proactively adapting to the changing energy landscape.

The Group's diversified strategy aligns with society's demand for a reliable, sustainable and costeffective energy mix. To support this strategy, MOTOR OIL Group has committed to a €4.0 billion investment program by 2030, with over €2.5 billion allocated to low-carbon energy projects and €1.5 billion dedicated to enhancing resilience and upgrading its infrastructure.

#### Refinery Optimization and Continuous Improvement

The Group's refinery plays a critical role in the energy transition by ensuring the production of essential fuels while integrating sustainability into its operations. To maintain efficiency, reliability and environmental responsibility, the Group continuously invests in projects aimed at optimizing refinery performance, upgrading product quality and reducing environmental impact. Key initiatives include:

1\. Completion of the Naphtha Processing Complex, enhancing the refinery's ability to produce highervalue products.

2\. Investment in a new propylene production unit, diversifying its portfolio beyond traditional fuelbased products.

3\. 160,000 tpa propylene production unit diversifying the refinery's product mix. Construction to be completed by 2025.

4\. Storage capacity expansion to be completed by 2025 (>40,000 m3).

5\. New port facilities (Athina) to materially expand processing, storage and trading capacity (>5 mn tons throughput potential).

6\. Development of >250,000 tpa advanced biofuel production program.

7\. Integration of 67 MW dependable efficient generation capacity to reinforce resilience.

8\. Implementation of digital transformation, energy efficiency and renewable energy projects to enhance operational performance.

#### E-Mobility and Retail Expansion

Through MOTOR OIL's subsidiaries, the Group offers integrated solutions across three key areas: Mobility, Convenience and Energy for Home & Business.. In Mobility, the Group installed 1,629 publicly accessible charging points by 2024 and is driving the transition to electric transportation by deploying over 4,000 EV charging stations by 2030, while continuing to expand its network of conventional service stations.. In Convenience, MOTOR OIL Group is redefining fuel stations as multi-service destinations by forming strategic partnerships with leading retail brands. In 2023, the Group introduced Smartshop standalone stores, offering a diverse range of products and services beyond traditional fuel stations. In 2024 two (2) standalone concept convenience stores are operating in Athens and 25 more to be added following the completion of the Twenty4Shopen transaction.. To enhance customer experience, the Group is developing the "gas station of the future", integrating advanced non-fuel services, low-carbon fuels and hydrogen.. Through MOTOR OIL's subsidiary NRG, the Group provides affordable energy solutions while promoting energy efficiency for homes and businesses. In 2024, 2.6 TWh of electricity are distributed to customers.

With these initiatives, the Group emphasizes on providing high customer experience, bringing innovative solutions and greater accessibility to energy services.



<!-- Report Page 88 -->
### Expansion in Renewable Energy

As a response to society's increasing demand for cleaner energy, the Group has established itself as one of the largest renewable energy producers in Greece and the broader region.. Over the past four years, MOTOR OIL Group have expanded operational capacity to 839 MW and developed a robust project pipeline 2.7 GW.. The Group's objective is to further strengthen MORE renewable energy business, with a target to surpass 2.0 GW in operational capacity and exceed €250 million in EBITDA by 2030.

Through these investments, the Group aims to solidify Group's position as a leading producer and provider of renewable energy in the region.

#### Alternative Fuels and Circular Economy

Leveraging Group's expertise in complex industrial processes, the Group is developing alternative fuels and expanding its circular economy initiatives to promote resource efficiency and sustainability.

#### Key focus areas include:. Natural Gas, Low-Carbon Hydrogen and Advanced Biofuels as part of MOTOR OIL Group alternative fuels' strategy.. Recycling, Waste Management, Biofuels Feedstock Development and Lubricant Regeneration, supporting the circular economy transition.

#### Recent milestones include:. Investment in biofuels through VERD and PRASINO LADI, focusing on biodiesel production and waste cooking oil/animal fats collection.

· Creation of HELLENIC HYDROGEN SA, a joint venture with PPC SA, developing Greece's first electrolysis projects for hydrogen production.. Integration of THALIS, enhancing capabilities in solid and liquid waste treatment, energy conservation and RES applications.

· Acquisition of HELECTOR enhancing of biogas production prospects up to 32 MW from waste treatment.. Advancement of the Blue Med program, which will play a key role in developing Greece's hydrogen economy, generating economic and environmental benefits while strengthening the country's energy security.. Operation of one of Southeastern Europe's largest lubricants refineries, through LPC, specializing in used lube oil regeneration and lubricant production.. Construction of the 877 MW CCGT Power Plant in Komotini, a state-of-the-art natural gas facility with efficiency levels of up to 64%. The plant is designed to integrate hydrogen as a fuel, ensuring longterm sustainability and competitiveness in a transitioning market. Developed in partnership with GEK Terna, the plant begun operations in 2024.

As part of its long-term commitment to sustainability, MOTOR OIL has set clear environmental, social and governance (ESG) goals to drive responsible growth and value creation. These objectives align with global sustainability trends, stakeholder expectations and regulatory requirements, ensuring that the Group continues to evolve in a way that benefits both the business and society. ### 1.4.3 Interests and Views of Stakeholders [SBM-2]

At MOTOR OIL Group, stakeholder engagement is an ongoing and fundamental process that fosters trust, transparency and mutual value creation. The Group defines stakeholders as all individuals, groups and entities that have an influence on, or are influenced by, its operations. This engagement is continuous throughout the year and is managed across multiple functions at the Group level to ensure effective communication and collaboration.

Recognizing the importance of strong relationships with stakeholders, MOTOR OIL Group companies actively maintain open and constructive dialogue with all key stakeholder groups. MOTOR OIL Group is dedicated to transparent reporting of sustainability performance, except for cases where confidentiality is required due to business-sensitive information. Given the scale and complexity of the operations and the broad network of stakeholders involved, engagement is an evolving and structured process aimed at identifying, addressing and mitigating potential and actual negative impacts.

A key priority has been the engagement of the Group's employees, who represent its most valuable asset. Beyond conducting materiality assessments, MOTOR OIL Group's workforce and Sustainability Committee are regularly involved in ESG-related discussions, ensuring they are informed about material sustainability issues and performance reviews.

Moreover, investors, suppliers and customers are kept up to date on Group's sustainability performance through the annual sustainability reports (the sustainability statement integrated in the Group's annual report from 2024 onwards). The Group also engages in reciprocal communication by responding to ESGrelated questionnaires from key stakeholders such as banks, investors, suppliers and clients, enabling an ongoing dialogue and ensuring alignment with stakeholder expectations.

Looking ahead, MOTOR OIL Group is committed to enhancing stakeholder engagement by expanding its consultation efforts to include a wider range of stakeholders in the target-setting process, ensuring that sustainability commitments effectively address potential negative impacts. As part of this initiative, the Group aims to strengthen its Supplier Code of Conduct and introduce ESG-specific questionnaires for suppliers, helping assess and manage risks and opportunities within the Group's value chain.

### Stakeholder Engagement and Business Model

MOTOR OIL Group continuously adapts its strategy and business model to align with sustainability goals and the evolving expectations of its stakeholders. The Group assesses and refines its approach through due diligence processes and materiality evaluations to ensure long-term resilience and competitiveness.

To address stakeholder interests and concerns, MOTOR OIL Group has introduced strategic modifications, focusing on enhancing operational efficiency, expanding into renewable energy and investing in lowcarbon technologies. Planned initiatives include further developments in electromobility infrastructure, biofuel production and energy storage solutions, with specific implementation timelines set to drive sustainable growth. These measures are expected to influence stakeholder relationships by fostering stronger engagement and trust. A key part of this approach is Internal and External Stakeholder Engagement.

MOTOR OIL Group recognizes that its own workforce is a key group of affected stakeholders and their interests, views and rights, including the respect for human rights, are integral to shaping the Group's strategy and business model. MOTOR OIL Group engages its employees through structured dialogues, feedback mechanisms and regular consultations, ensuring their voices are considered in decision-making processes. The Group actively integrates workforce-related insights into its strategic planning, fostering an inclusive work environment and reinforcing its commitment to fair labor practices and human rights protections. This approach not only supports employee well-being but also strengthens the Group's long-term resilience and sustainable growth strategy.

Additionally, MOTOR OIL Group places great emphasis on regular consultations with local communities, regulatory bodies and non-governmental organizations (NGOs) to ensure inclusive decision-making that considers the concerns and needs of those affected by its operations. This continuous dialogue helps strengthen relationships and ensures that the Group remains responsive to external expectations and requirements. Additionally, MOTOR OIL Group has established an Incident Reporting Framework to ensure transparency and accountability. By openly reporting any negative impacts or incidents to stakeholders and the public, the Group fosters trust and demonstrates its commitment to addressing issues in a timely and responsible manner.

MOTOR OIL Group ensures that its governance bodies, including executive management and supervisory boards, remain well-informed about stakeholder perspectives and sustainability-related impacts. This is achieved through regular reporting, stakeholder consultations and integrating sustainability considerations into strategic decision-making processes.

#### 1.4.4 Material Impacts, Risks and Opportunities and their interaction with Strategy and Business Model [SBM-3]

MOTOR OIL Group's double materiality assessment has revealed a range of material impacts, risks and opportunities that reflect the nature of its integrated multi-energy operations and its strategic transition toward sustainability. These material issues are concentrated across the upstream, own operations and downstream stages of the Group's business model and value chain.

In the upstream segment, material topics are associated with the procurement and use of natural resources, including crude oil, natural gas, feedstocks, water and energy inputs. The sourcing and transportation of these materials, along with reliance on electricity, water and chemicals for refining and environmental projects, create material environmental risks. Additionally, the use of financial capital to fund large-scale infrastructure, renewable projects and remediation efforts introduces financial and strategic exposure.

Within own operations, the Group's refining activities account for many of its material negative environmental and social impacts. These include greenhouse gas emissions, air and water pollution, use of hazardous substances and occupational health and safety risks due to the inherent complexity

and hazards of refining and energy generation processes. However, this is also where the Group's most transformative sustainability opportunities are concentrated, such as investment in renewable energy, the circular economy, innovation and decarbonization. Material positive impacts stem from actions such as co-processing biofuels, producing low-carbon hydrogen, regenerating lubricants, managing waste and installing renewable power generation and storage infrastructure at the site level.

The downstream value chain contains risks related to emissions from fuel consumption, product lifecycle environmental effects and biodiversity concerns tied to infrastructure development such as wind parks. On the opportunity side, customer-facing platforms, including retail fuel stations, electric vehicle (EV) charging infrastructure and non-fuel services, offer pathways to decarbonized transport and sustainability-linked customer engagement. Circular initiatives - such as used oil collection and lubricant regeneration - extend sustainability beyond the consumer phase, into end-of-life product handling and recycling.

The presence of these impacts and risks has influenced MOTOR OIL's Group strategic trajectory. Over the past two years, the Group has significantly expanded its activities in renewable energy, low-carbon fuels and circular economy solutions. Key strategic shifts include large-scale investments in solar and wind generation, green hydrogen, carbon capture and storage technologies and waste management infrastructure. These investments are reshaping MOTOR OIL's long-term asset portfolio and resource allocation, aligning the business model with the anticipated demands of a decarbonized economy and future regulatory conditions.

The Group's material negative impacts directly affect people and the environment through emissions and industrial hazards. Conversely, its positive impacts include improved air and water quality, enhanced biodiversity through reforestation programs and stakeholder value creation via local investment and workforce development.

Most environmental and climate-related impacts and opportunities are considered long-term, such as GHG reduction through CCS and hydrogen investments while innovation- and customer-related opportunities may yield benefits over mid- to long-term horizons. The Group is directly involved in these impacts through its operations and indirectly through its suppliers, contractors and customers. For example, Scope 3 emissions, biodiversity effects from infrastructure projects and lifecycle product impacts reflect indirect linkages to the Group's broader ecosystem.

Financially, these material risks and opportunities already exert tangible effects. The Group has quantified their impact in terms of required capital deployment, earnings sensitivity and long-term value creation potential. The transition toward a lower-carbon, more diversified energy portfolio requires significant investment, with up to €2.5 billion planned for energy transition initiatives, including renewable infrastructure, low-carbon fuels and decarbonization technologies. While transition-related risks, such as rising compliance costs and technology costs, could impact EBITDA in the near term, MOTOR OIL's Group strategy is focused on capturing upside from non-fossil activities, which are targeted to contribute more than 40% of group EBITDA by 2030.

The resilience of the Group's business model has been shaped by and continues to evolve in response to sustainability matters. Through its diversified portfolio, digital transformation initiatives and operational flexibility, the Group is actively adapting to sustainability-related risks and impacts while capturing emerging opportunities. Key environmental and social factors - particularly those related to the lowcarbon transition - are embedded into strategic decision-making and business planning. As such, sustainability considerations influence the Group's strategy, enabling MOTOR OIL to strengthen its longterm financial performance while contributing to climate goals and broader sustainability outcomes.

The tables that follow provide brief descriptions of these significant impacts and risks. More detailed information on how the Group manages these issues is provided in the relevant sections of this report.

### 1.5 Impacts, Risks and Opportunities Management

#### 1.5.1 Description of the process to Identify and Assess Material Impacts, Risks and Opportunities [IRO-1]

MOTOR OIL Group has conducted a Double Materiality Assessment (DMA) to identify, reassess and prioritize the critical areas of sustainability. This includes the impact materiality, which examines the Group's effects on society, governance and the environment and at the same time, financial materiality, meaning the identification of significant risks and opportunities that affect (or could reasonably be expected to affect) the financial position, financial performance, cash flows, access to funding, or the cost of capital in the short, medium, or long term.

##### Step 1: Creating the inventory of impacts, risks and opportunities

The first phase of MOTOR OIL Group's Double Materiality Assessment focused on a benchmarking analysis to build an understanding of the Group's material topics, both specific to its operations and the broader industry. This process involved a comparative analysis, where MOTOR OIL Group examined its operations alongside those of its industry peers, thus evaluating key sustainability themes relevant to the Group and the sector. Additionally, Group revisited material topics identified in previous assessments, ensuring these remained applicable to the current sustainability landscape, which has evolved with shifting environmental and social priorities.

Therefore, MOTOR OIL Group's approach was structured around three key activities: an analysis of specific sub-topics, a benchmarking study of peer practices and a review of existing material topics informed by the CSRD. This benchmarking also incorporated insights from impact materiality identified in prior reports, aiming to provide a consistent framework for sustainability evaluation. To strengthen alignment with the CSRD, MOTOR OIL Group's assessment included a review of the sector specific CSRD Standards, refining the focus areas to better address the unique impacts and sustainability considerations of the industry.

The assessment places particular focus on activities and business relationships that may present a heightened risk of adverse impacts. These include operations in high-impact sectors such as refining, energy infrastructure and petrochemicals, as well as regions where environmental stress or social risks may be elevated. Specific attention is also given to business activities involving hazardous materials, high energy intensity and land use, such as the development of wind parks or energy-from-waste facilities. Through this targeted approach, the Group is able to flag and monitor areas with disproportionate environmental or social exposure.

The process explicitly considers the impacts that originate both from MOTOR OIL's own operations and those that arise through its business relationships, including supply chain partners, customers and joint ventures. For example, emissions and occupational safety risks are directly linked to in-house operations, while product use-phase emissions or biodiversity impacts from infrastructure projects may result from partnerships or indirect involvement. This perspective ensures that the Group accounts for its full sphere of influence when prioritizing and addressing sustainability impacts.

###### Value Chain mapping

MOTOR OIL Group's impact identification, assessment and prioritization process is closely integrated with its sustainability due diligence framework. As part of its due diligence, the Group conducted a value chain analysis to determine where significant environmental, social and economic impacts are most likely to occur. This assessment covered upstream, own operations and downstream stages-focusing on key business activities such as sourcing, production, distribution and product end-use.

The process evaluated the severity and likelihood of impacts across each stage of the value chain, with emphasis placed on areas where the Group has greater operational control and influence. Notably, own operations and downstream activities were identified as the primary areas of material impact, particularly concerning environmental and safety issues. In contrast, certain upstream social risks - such as human rights or child labor - were considered less likely or severe due to the Group's indirect involvement in those areas. Nonetheless, these were still addressed through policies such as the Supplier Code of Conduct and other due diligence measures.

This approach, which aligns with ESRS, ensures that impact assessment is not only data-driven but also risk-informed and context-specific. By embedding it within the broader due diligence process, the Group has developed a more streamlined, accountable and resilient framework for identifying and managing sustainability-related risks and opportunities across its value chain.



<!-- Report Page 100 -->
###### Stakeholder mapping

MOTOR OIL Group developed a dynamic stakeholder map to understand the influence and impact of various stakeholders on its operations. Key groups were identified and categorized based on the ESRS requirements: Affected Stakeholders and Users of Sustainability Statements. The group employed diverse engagement methods, such as communication channels, workshops and feedback mechanisms, to facilitate ongoing dialogue with each group, across all group operations and subsidiaries. Each stakeholder group was assigned a priority level, enabling MOTOR OIL Group to allocate resources effectively for engagement; this mapping provided insights into stakeholders' concerns, expectations and potential areas of collaboration or conflict. Thus, the Group ensured that stakeholder perspectives were integrated into strategic decision-making, enhancing its overall sustainability and risk management approach, while also creating the necessary background for easier identification of impacts, risks and opportunities.

The key stakeholder groups identified include employees, business partners and suppliers, clients and consumers, shareholders, the investment and financial community, industry peers, media, NGOs and local communities, unions and universities and the scientific community.
