# 7 RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR ECONOMY [E5]

## 7.1 Waste policies, actions and targets

### 7.1.1 Policies and actions related to resource use and circular economy [E5-1], [E5-2]

#### Policies on Waste and Resource Use

MOTOR OIL Group integrates sustainable waste management as a core pillar of its broader circular economy strategy, aiming to reduce environmental impact while enhancing resource efficiency across its value chain. The Group's waste policy reflects a forward-thinking approach grounded in the principles of the Best Available Techniques (BAT) and circular economy and is implemented through a certified Environmental Management System in accordance with ISO 14001.

This policy framework ensures the responsible handling of hazardous and non-hazardous waste, promotes waste prevention and supports recovery and reuse practices wherever possible. The Group's activities include:

· Lubricant regeneration, reducing the need for virgin materials. Used Cooking Oil (UCO) collection to support biofuel production

· Municipal wastewater treatment, aiding water conservation and reuse. Waste-to-energy and mechanical-biological treatment (MBT) solutions, aimed at reducing landfill dependency

The Group aligns its policies, supervised by Sustainability Committee, with national and European legislation, such as Law 4042/2012 in compliance with 2008/98/EU, continuously monitoring its compliance through regular EHS audits, performance monitoring and stakeholder collaboration by providing consultation, information and training to employees, contractors and other staff working on its behalf in order to ensure their commitment to safety and awareness.

#### Actions Taken on Circular Economy activitie

In line with its policy commitments, MOTOR OIL Group has launched a range of strategic initiatives and investments to support sustainable waste and resource management:

Expansion of Waste Infrastructure: The Group is scaling up its waste processing capacity through investments in Energy-from-Waste (EfW) and MBT facilities, allowing greater throughput and reducing landfilling.

UCO Collection Scaling: Through partnerships with the hospitality sector and investments in logistics, the Group is expanding its network for Used Cooking Oil collection to support circular biofuel production.

Lubricant Regeneration Upgrades: Existing facilities are being modernized to handle higher volumes and improve recovery efficiency, transforming waste into high-value secondary materials.

Municipal Wastewater Treatment Expansion: Modular, scalable wastewater treatment technologies are being deployed in collaboration with local authorities, improving cost-efficiency and service coverage.

Integrated Circular Economy Platform: MOTOR OIL Group operates a comprehensive circular economy platform that encompasses solid waste management, lubricant regeneration and municipal wastewater treatment.

These actions reflect MOTOR OIL Group's commitment to a resilient, low-carbon and circular economy, while also addressing key environmental risks associated with industrial waste, such as soil and water contamination, biodiversity loss and long-term remediation costs.

#### Refinery, Supply and Trading activities

At the heart of MOTOR OIL Group's environmental strategy is an integrated waste management system that is embedded within the refinery's operational framework. Every stage of waste handling - classification, collection, processing and final disposal - is meticulously managed to comply with environmental regulations and uphold MOTOR OIL Group's sustainability commitments. Refinery operations are central to Group's circularity strategy, focusing on reducing waste. Investments in advanced waste treatment infrastructure, including a planned hazardous waste facility, align with cutting-edge techniques like biopiling to manage hydrocarbon-contaminated soils.

Maximizing resource recovery remains a key priority within MOTOR OIL Group operations. Materials such as used catalysts undergo regeneration and metal reclamation through accredited third parties, contributing to the circular economy. Also, the Group assesses the possibility of repurposing oil sludge and petroleum residues for energy uses, instead of being discarded, supporting industrial circularity and minimizing environmental impact. To close material loops, it also contributes to the circular economy by utilizing spent catalyst as fuel in the metal industry. Additionally, sulfur recovery via Claus units reinforces sustainability objectives, ensuring that all processes contribute to greater efficiency and reduced waste generation.

Beyond conventional waste treatment, the Group employs biopiling, a BAT bioremediation technique that restores hydrocarbon-contaminated soils, further demonstrating its commitment to sustainability. During the reporting period, no significant amounts of CapEx and OpEx were dedicated. Waste processing is carried out in collaboration with certified partners who follow a strict framework, ensuring that materials are either recycled, repurposed, or disposed of responsibly and in compliance with regulations. More specifically, these partners are required by MOTOR OIL's procedure to be licensed according to relevant legislation and registered on Electronic Waste Registry for collection, transfer and management of hazardous waste for each type of waste. MOTOR OIL Group commitment to circularity extends across all operations, with a continuous drive for improvement that integrates innovation with environmental stewardship, ensuring that sustainability remains at the forefront of its strategy.

By refurbishing and maintaining refinery equipment, the Group extends equipment lifecycles, minimize waste generation and preserve valuable resources. These initiatives, alongside circular water usage and the integration of co-processed biodiesel into refinery fuel, further advance MOTOR OIL Group's commitment to reducing emissions and promoting renewable energy solutions.

#### Retail activities

Across Group's retail operations, waste minimization remains a priority. MOTOR OIL Group proactively reduces hazardous materials in production, service stations and offices, embedding sustainability into every aspect of the business. AVIN ensures responsible lubricant and packaging management through licensed companies, while CORAL GAS advances recycling efforts by repurposing tin plate sheet residues and recovering LPG from non-compliant cartridges and cylinders. Beyond individual initiatives, the Group reinforces sustainable practices through participation in national recycling programs, fostering a culture of environmental responsibility among employees and partners.

Recognizing the significance of waste management at all levels, the Group has implemented an Integrated Recycling Management System within the offices at Group's HQ, ensuring that solid waste is sorted at the source and that zero quantity of waste generated is directed to disposal. Staff training plays a key role in this initiative - by empowering its teams with the knowledge and tools to manage waste efficiently, the Group enhances its collective impact on sustainability.

<!-- Report Page 212 -->
#### Circular Economy and Alternative Fuels activities

Circular economic principles are integral to Group's approach to resource utilization and waste reduction, ensuring sustainability throughout the Group's operations. Through VERD, the Group contributes to sustainable energy solutions by directing sludge derived from wastewater to third parties' biogas plants, effectively eliminating solid waste. Any remaining effluents undergo a thorough oil separation process before being transferred to local treatment facilities. This focus on resource recovery is part of a broader commitment to waste minimization, ensuring that each step of waste handling - from collection to final disposal - aligns with MOTOR OIL Group's sustainability goals. By reprocessing waste materials and diverting them from landfills, the Group maximizes resource efficiency and reduce environmental impact.

PRASINO LADI strengthens Group's dedication to alternative fuels by collecting used cooking oils, which would otherwise be improperly disposed of and sending it to VERD for biodiesel production processing. This cleaner, renewable fuel contributes to emissions reductions and enhances the sustainability of its operations. This initiative is a key part of Group's circular economy strategy, supporting both the reduction of waste and the promotion of innovative, fuel alternatives. Similarly, LPC's regeneration of rerefined lubricants from waste lube oil (WLO) helps close resource loops, providing high-quality lubricants that are both sold domestically and exported internationally. These efforts further reduce waste while contributing to sustainable industrial practices.

THALIS further strengthens the Group's sustainability commitment through detailed waste management practices across its offices and construction sites. On construction sites, waste management is documented through invoices and waste delivery receipts for materials such as WEEE and lubricating oils. Non-hazardous solid waste, including paper, plastic, metals and wood, is responsibly managed alongside other office-related waste, supporting a comprehensive and transparent waste management system that ensures proper disposal and resource recovery.

Beyond these individual programs, the Group is actively engaged in European initiatives promoting sustainable development and waste recovery. Hazardous waste is managed through licensed companies, ensuring compliance and preventing contamination of municipal waste systems. Throughout its operations, it recovers products from refining processes that deviate from specifications and reprocess them wherever feasible, minimizing waste and enhancing resource efficiency. A comprehensive Recycling Management System is in place across all facilities, focusing on waste sorting at the source and integrating employee training to foster a culture of environmental responsibility.

Innovation is at the forefront of Group's waste management strategy. Participation in research projects in 2024 such as WASTEMILL, MAESTRO and HYDROGAS showcases Group's commitment to developing waste-to-value solutions. These initiatives explore new ways to transform organic municipal waste into valuable raw materials, optimize biofuel production and enhance energy recovery. Additionally, subsidiaries such as THALIS contribute to public engagement by raising awareness about circular economy practices, with initiatives such as the 2024 School Recycling Marathon further reinforcing Group's collaborative approach to sustainability. By continually integrating circular economy principles, the Group redefines sustainability, balancing operational excellence with environmental responsibility and aiming to positively impact future generations.

## 7.1.2 Targets related to resource use and circular economy [E5-3]

As part of Group's commitment to sustainability and the circular economy, its target by 2026 is to significantly scale up its waste management, renewable feedstock utilization and energy recovery capabilities across its operations. By that time, MOTOR OIL Group aims to manage over 300,000 tonnes of solid waste annually and municipal wastewater treatment capacity over 15 million cubic meters per year.

In the area of renewable feedstock, the Group plans to collect more than 28,000 tonnes of used cooking oil (UCO) annually. MOTOR OIL Group is also committed to increasing lubricant processing and regeneration capacity to over 43,000 tonnes per year. Finally, through strategic expansion of landfill gas and mechanical-biological treatment (MBT) capabilities, Group aims to develop over 35 MW of biogasbased energy recovery capacity, contributing significantly to Group's renewable energy portfolio.

While MOTOR OIL Group does not set specific targets related to waste from own operations, it's dedicated to responsible waste management and minimization practices in line with environmental regulations and circular economy principles. The Group actively implements measures to reduce, reuse and recycle waste, promotes the safe handling and disposal of hazardous materials and continuously seeks opportunities to enhance waste efficiency across its operations.

# 7.2 Metrics

## 7.2.1 Resource outflows [E5-5]

From oil and lubricant operations to packaging and industrial processes, waste takes many forms - each requiring careful handling. Sludges from oil/water separators, along with interceptor sludges and aqueous liquid wastes, are part of the broader challenge of liquid waste, demanding specialized treatment methods. At the same time, contaminated absorbents, filter materials and protective equipment reflect the operational realities of handling hazardous substances.

Generally waste streams from Group's facilities consist of: Oily solid waste (sludge), used catalysts from process units, resins, activated carbon, paraffin, waste lubricating oils (WLO), waste electrical and electronic equipment (WEEE), excavation, construction and demolition waste (ECSC), packages containing residues of dangerous substances, metal materials (scrap), absorbent materials and clothing that have been contaminated (oily rags), packaging materials, municipal solid waste (paper, plastics, food waste, etc.), laboratory waste, medical waste.

Across facilities, the Group encounters a diverse mix of materials, from end-of-life tires and vehicle components to lead batteries and spent catalysts. The refining process itself generates catalysts and transition metal-containing residues. Metals such as aluminum, iron and steel emerge from various activities, while plastic waste, cables and construction debris contribute to the complex waste landscape.

MOTOR OIL Group recognizes that packaging - whether paper, cardboard, plastic, or metal - plays a crucial role in logistics and product integrity, yet it also contributes to its waste footprint. Some of them contain hazardous residues, necessitating stricter handling. Similarly, the management of used oils, mineral-based lubricants and other engine fluids demands meticulous oversight, ensuring that what fuels industries do not burden the environment.

Beyond industrial sites, waste from discarded electrical equipment and fluorescent tubes reflects the breadth of MOTOR OIL Group's operations. While some waste streams are expected, others, such as sulphur and bitumen byproducts, highlight the unexpected intersections between sectors. Throughout it all, the Group remains committed to tracking, documenting and diverting waste from disposal wherever possible. Managing waste is not just an obligation, it is integral to how the Group operates, balancing efficiency with environmental responsibility.

<!-- Report Page 214 -->
### Circular Economy and Alternative Fuels activities

VERD, as part of the Group's broader sustainability strategy, focuses on biofuel production by utilizing recycled materials such as used vegetable oils and animal fats. By processing these materials into biofuels, VERD furthers the Group's commitment to the circular economy. This process not only reduces waste but also promotes sustainability by lowering the environmental impact associated with traditional fuel production, reinforcing the Group's role in driving innovation in clean energy solutions.

LPC contributes to the circular economy through its specialization in regeneration of lubricants, offering a variety of products including base oils, blended lubricants (both bulk and packaged), greases and related by-products. The Group places a strong emphasis on resource efficiency and environmental responsibility, aiming that every stage of its lubricant production process aligns with sustainability goals. By processing waste lubricants and repurposing them into high-quality products, LPC supports a circular lifecycle for lubricants, reducing waste and promoting long-term sustainability within the industry. Indicatively, LPC has put into its regeneration process 34,137 MT of WLO in 2024.

Waste generated is classified according to European Waste Catalogue (Decision 2014/955/EE) and the relevant data derive from direct measurements. Disposal or recycling management methods take place exclusively by licensed third parties and outside the Group's facilities.

As far as operational sites are concerned, raw data of waste generated and presented in the above tables are submitted to the National Registry on an annual basis. During the submission process, entities that manage waste are required to submit quantities of hazardous and non-hazardous waste generated, collected, transferred and disposed/recycled. Therefore, all waste is tracked. Also, the quantities for Motor Oil refinery and LPC are validated under Eco Management and Audit Scheme (EMAS) annually. Finally, as far as Motor Oil's HQ and retail companies' offices integrated management system is concerned, waste collection, treatment and reporting are conducted by a certified third party. The KPI's presented are subjected to potential changes until final submission.

<!-- Report Page 218 -->
#### 8 OWN WORKFORCE [S1]

##### 8.1 Strategy

###### 8.1.1 Material Impacts, Risks and Opportunities and their interaction with Strategy and Business Model [S1.SBM-3]

MOTOR OIL's Group strategy and business model, underscore its commitment to safeguarding its workforce by proactively addressing both existing and potential Impacts, Risks and Opportunities (IROs) as presented in Chapter: Material impacts, risks and opportunities [SBM-3]. The material risks and opportunities arising from impacts and dependencies are closely related to the strategy and business model of MOTOR OIL Group. Group's business model and strategy are guided by the principles and values of responsibility towards its employees in the realm of equal opportunities as well as occupational health and safety at work. Further information on Group's principles and values can be found in the Code of Ethics and Corporate Responsibility.

The Group has assessed a negative material impact on Health & Safety that stems from the inherent nature of oil and gas industry, including explosions, fires, chemical leaks which pose significant environmental and human health impacts. Nevertheless, in high-risk operations such as refining, rigorous safety protocols not only support the protection of employees but also foster trust among shareholders and unions. Additionally, extended working hours in sectors such as refining, retail, circular economy & alternative fuels and RES necessitate robust support systems to mitigate fatigue.

These challenges are met with policies, from equal opportunity initiatives ensuring fair hiring and career advancement to worker rights associations advocating for better conditions, particularly in refining and fuels sector. Professional development is supported by continuous training programs enhancing employability across sectors, complementing efforts to foster an inclusive workforce. At the same time, workplace harassment policies contribute to fostering a respectful environment, while comprehensive medical programs are also provided. These initiatives reinforce a culture in which safety and well-being are prioritized both within and beyond the workplace.

MOTOR OIL Group recognizes that its own workforce is a key group of affected stakeholders (further analysis regarding the stakeholders can be found in sub-Chapter: Interests and views of stakeholders [SBM-2]) and their interests, views and rights, including the respect for human rights, are integral to shaping the Group's strategy and business model. MOTOR OIL Group engages its employees through structured dialogues, feedback mechanisms and regular consultations. The Group actively integrates workforce-related insights into its strategic planning, fostering an inclusive work environment and reinforcing its commitment to fair labor practices and protection of human rights. This approach not only supports employee well-being but also strengthens the Group's long-term resilience and sustainable growth strategy.

Operating in an Oil & Gas sector, employees within MOTOR OIL Group face inherent challenges tied to the complexity of refining processes, the management of volatile substances and the potential for severe incidents such as explosions, fires, chemical leaks and falls. These risks, embedded in the sector, require constant vigilance, as safety must coexist with the operational demands of companies within the refining, retail, circular economy and alternative fuels and Renewables sectors. The unpredictability of hazardous environments underscores the need for a structured and proactive approach, ensuring that risk mitigation is not just a regulatory obligation but an integral part of daily operations.

The Group remains committed to safety protocols aligned with international standards, prioritizing measures that protect employees and maintain operational integrity. At the same time, the Group actively explores additional safeguards, including the integration of advanced safety technologies and the establishment of a competent health and safety committee, a body that serves as a focal point for identifying emerging hazards, reinforcing best practices and make efforts towards compliance with evolving regulations.

Beyond immediate operational safety, MOTOR OIL Group through its Code of Ethics and Corporate Responsibility takes a broader view of workforce well-being, to mitigate certain labor risks across operations and supply chains. While no material risks of forced or child labor have been identified, the adherence to the code allows the Group to proactively address geographic and operational vulnerabilities. Contractors and outsourced providers are encouraged to adhere to labor compliance practices aligned with international standards, reinforcing ethical employment practices and ensuring fair working conditions. The Group also recognizes that employee well-being extends beyond the workplace. Parental leave policies are designed to provide stability during key life transitions, with maternity leave fully covered by the employer before transitioning to state-subsidized extensions, while paternity leave ensures fathers can balance professional and family responsibilities.

By embedding proactive safety and workforce initiatives, MOTOR OIL Group strengthens both employee security and operational resilience. At the same time, the Group ensures that its strategic approach aligns with broader sustainability objectives, reinforcing stakeholder confidence and long-term performance.

####### Risks and Opportunities

Beyond operational challenges, MOTOR OIL Group navigates a complex landscape of organizational risks that directly influence long-term financial stability and growth. The capital-intensive nature of oil and gas operations demands constant oversight of potential liabilities, ranging from regulatory compliance costs to operational incidents that could lead to financial penalties, insurance cost increases and investor uncertainty. The Group mitigates these exposures through safety frameworks designed to prevent costly disruptions and safeguard operational continuity. By reinforcing risk management mechanisms, the Group maintains financial predictability, ensuring that profitability remains resilient even in a volatile regulatory and market environment.

Market expectations surrounding corporate responsibility and workplace policies also introduce financial considerations. The Group recognizes that an inclusive and diverse workforce is not just a social commitment but a financial safeguard against inefficiencies. A failure to address diversity-related risks could lead to higher turnover costs, recruitment difficulties and reputational damage, all of which carry financial consequences. Workplace safety and well-being are not only fundamental operational concerns but also key financial levers. Establishing a competent health and safety committee strengthens the Group's ability to identify and address risks early, reducing incident-related downtimes, regulatory fines and insurance liabilities. A safer work environment contributes to fewer operational disruptions, improved cost control and a lower risk profile - factors that directly support valuation strength and investor confidence. As the Group evaluates long-term growth strategies, the shift toward greener and climateneutral operations presents both risks and financial opportunities. Transitioning to sustainable practices requires significant investment, but failing to adapt could expose the Group to regulatory penalties, carbon pricing mechanisms and shifting consumer preferences, all of which could undermine market positioning and ultimately impact its own workforce.

The broader workforce benefits (such as long-term incentive plans and special recognition awards, health check-ups, company-funded post graduate scholarships, family perks (eg. Childcare support) and case-based benefits including food cards/coupons/tickets restaurant) also serve as financial stabilizers, ensuring long-term employee retention and reducing the hidden costs of turnover, such as recruitment, onboarding and lost productivity.

Another avenue for financial resilience lies in worker representation through the Unions. Supporting a dedicated worker rights association and respecting the right to freely join trade unions and professional associations, mitigates risks associated with labor disputes, which can lead to costly work stoppages, legal proceedings and reputational harm. By fostering structured engagement channels such discussions, surveys and feedback mechanisms, the Group enhances internal stability, preserving business continuity and protecting revenue streams from disruption.

Investment in training and development further strengthens financial positioning by supporting the workforce remains equipped to meet evolving industry demands. Technical upskilling programs not only improve operational efficiency but also bridge knowledge gaps, increase productivity and quality, that could otherwise slow the Group's adaptation to emerging market trends. As sustainability imperatives reshape the industry, proactively aligning workforce capabilities with future operational needs safeguards long-term financial viability.

<!-- Report Page 220 -->
####### Types of own workforce

MOTOR OIL Group's workforce consists of both employees and non-employees who are subject to material impact due to the nature of their work. Employees include permanent staff and temporary employees, primarily engaged in office-based roles at the headquarters and operational positions across various sites (field-based). Non-employees, recorded at the end of the reporting period, mainly consist of contractors and individuals provided by Temporary Employment Agencies. These workers are predominantly engineers and their teams, as well as personnel employed at retail stations. Their work is primarily manual and field-based, involving operations within the refinery and retail locations. Given the demanding nature of these roles, workplace safety, fair employment conditions and adequate protections remain key priorities for the Group. All individuals within MOTOR OIL Group's workforce may be affected by the material impacts and opportunities previously outlined, unless otherwise indicated. The Group is dedicated to fostering an inclusive workplace where all employees have equal opportunities for professional growth and advancement.

##### 8.2 Impact, risk and opportunity management

###### 8.2.1 Policies and Actions related to Equal Treatment and Opportunities for All [S1-1] [$1-4]

####### Diversity

MOTOR OIL Group, unwavering its commitment to fostering a fair and inclusive workplace, ensuring equal opportunities for all employees and job applicants. Group's policies such as on Rewards & Recognition, Internal Career Opportunities, Training & Development, Talent Acquisition & Recruitment, are guided by principles of equality, non-discrimination and respect for employee dignity.

The Code of Ethics and Corporate Responsibility serves as a key resource, offering guidance on professional behavior in daily operations. No type of racial discrimination or discrimination related to gender, religion, age, nationality, social or national origin, disability, beliefs, sexual orientation, or trade unionism is tolerated. These principles apply and are implemented starting with the recruitment of new employees, as well as throughout the cooperation and professional development of MOTOR OIL Group's people. A gender-neutral work environment ensures that hiring decisions are based on merit, with zero tolerance for sexual harassment or discrimination. While equal opportunity practices, as outlined in Group's "Code of Ethics and Corporate Responsibility", apply across all operations, the specialized nature and skill requirements of refinery work have historically led to a lower percentage of female applicants. To address this, the Group is actively implementing targeted initiatives - such as partnerships with technical university programs. Additionally, the Group engages with NGOs and campaigns that promote women's empowerment in local communities, such as "Women On Top" (womenontop.gr), to encourage greater female participation in engineering roles.

MOTOR OIL Group has implemented several policies (e.g. Internal Career Opportunities policy, DEI Policy, Talent Acquisition & Recruitment Policy) to promote equal opportunities and mitigate gender and racial disparities in hiring and promotions. The "Internal Career Opportunities Policy" ensures that all group employees have equal access to internal career opportunities across all subsidiaries and general divisions of the Group, offering opportunities for lateral moves, career advancement and career change. This policy reflects its commitment to social causes by emphasizing diversity, equity and inclusion throughout the entire internal hiring process.

The "Talent Acquisition & Recruitment Policy" establishes objective and merit-based recruitment practices, in line with MOTOR OIL Group's commitment to fairness and equal opportunities for all candidates. Candidate assessment is conducted through a structured process, including CV screening, phone interviews, online assessments and competency-based interviews. This ensures that hiring decisions are based on merit and competency rather than bias.

The "Learning & Development Policy" applies to all employees excluding those with definite contracts, interns, external partners and contractors, except where mandatory training is required. Refinery employees operate under a separate Learning & Development Policy that follows the same guiding principles.

Additionally, there is a "Diversity, Equity and Inclusion Policy (DEI Policy) " which reinforces the Group's commitment to fairness, transparency and respect in the workplace by setting clear guidelines for inclusive talent management and professional development. The Group has developed a framework for DEI initiatives and programs, which are detailed within the policy. The Learning & Development Policy reinforces inclusivity by ensuring equal access to learning development opportunities, fully aligning with the company's core values.

MOTOR OIL Group remains committed to fostering a workplace culture that prioritizes equity and inclusion while promoting psychological safety and upholding fundamental human rights. In support of this commitment, mechanisms have been established under the Whistleblowing Policy, providing employees with a secure and confidential process for reporting concerns. The company encourages the submission of anonymous or identifiable reports regarding any illegal conduct through its designated reporting channels, fostering a transparent and accountable work environment.

To ensure compliance with its DEI agenda, the Group has established framework aligned with the EU Corporate Sustainability Reporting Directive (CSRD). Moreover, adherence to internationally recognized frameworks, as incorporated into Greek legislation, such as the UN Guiding Principles on Business and Human Rights, the ILO Declaration on Fundamental Principles and Rights at Work and the OECD Guidelines for Multinational Enterprises which respectfully further strengthens the company's commitment to a transparent, fair and inclusive work environment. The Whistleblowing Policy also supports compliance with relevant Greek legislation, specifically Law No. 4990/2022 and Law No. 4808/2021, by covering specific types of misconduct.

In developing these policies, key stakeholders, including the Compliance Department, Legal Department and Sustainability division, have conducted thorough reviews and approvals. Their formulation has incorporated input from employees, HR professionals, senior executives and DEI specialists, ensuring alignment with best practices and organizational objectives. Regular employee surveys and feedback mechanisms contribute to refining these policies, addressing concerns related to equal opportunities, career advancement and professional development. Engagement with external partners, such as educational institutions and professional organizations, further strengthens the company's dedication to fostering an inclusive workplace.

The Group ensures that these policies are accessible to all relevant stakeholders. Employees can access them through internal communication channels, including the company's intranet (SharePoint) and dedicated training sessions, while external stakeholders can refer to them via the company's official website. During the onboarding process, new hires receive detailed briefings on relevant policies as

part of their induction program. Additionally, training sessions on the Code of Ethics and Corporate Responsibility and the Whistleblowing Policy are assigned to employees to reinforce compliance and awareness. Representatives from Human Resources department remain available to provide further guidance and ongoing training initiatives continue to promote understanding and adherence to these policies throughout the organization.

In addition, MOTOR OIL Group is committed to offering internal programs focused on strengthening the Group's culture and enhancing employees' understanding of Diversity, Equity and Inclusion (DEI). These initiatives aim to create a more inclusive and equitable work environment. Indicative programs and actions include continuous monitoring of diversity related metrics. Part of these metrics are pay equity, the share of women in the total workforce, the share of women in management positions (i.e. top management) and employees per gender within MOTOR OIL Group.

Lastly, MOTOR OIL Group integrates its dedication to DEI into the core of its Leadership Development Programs. These programs and initiatives are meticulously designed to instill DEI values in emerging leaders, ensuring that the principles of inclusivity, fairness and diversity are championed by future organizational leaders. This approach ensures that the Group's practices do not cause or contribute to material negative impacts on its workforce.

##### Human Rights

MOTOR OIL Group upholds high standards of integrity, fully adhering to applicable laws and regulations while respecting fundamental human rights. Group's commitment extends beyond compliance, embedding ethical labor practices, health and safety, security and environmental protection into the fabric of its operations. MOTOR OIL Group takes pride in being a signatory to the UN Global Compact, embracing its ten principles with respecting and abiding to the United Nations Declaration of Human Rights.

Promoting a workplace free from discrimination, the Group fosters an inclusive environment where factors such as gender, age, religion, marital status, racial or ethnic origin and sexual orientation have no bearing on compensation or career progression. MOTOR OIL Group's structured Rewards & Recognition Policy creates a fair and meritocratic approach to remuneration, reinforcing salary equity through a grade-based structure that aligns roles with their market value and impact.

Across all geographies where the Group operates, national legislation on child and forced labor is strictly observed. Such practices have never been condoned within Group's operations or those of its subsidiaries. Prevention is paramount and the Group actively oversees the evolving directive on corporate sustainability due diligence, equipping the Group with tools to identify, mitigate and address potential human rights and environmental impacts across MOTOR OIL Group's value chain. Among the responsibilities of the Sustainabitliy Division, is to ensure Group-wide compliance with ESG regulations, policies and frameworks, including climate-related obligations. It oversees the implementation of relevant ESG standards and stays informed on emerging legislation and industry best practices to maintain regulatory readiness.

A safe and respectful workplace is foundational to who the Group is. MOTOR OIL Group's Policy Against Violence and Harassment reflects this, addressing material risks and ensuring dignity at work. In alignment with local legislation (Law 4808/2021 and P.D. 80/2022), the Group takes decisive measures to prevent and respond to workplace violence, including gender-based and sexual harassment. Risk assessments, preventive measures and incident investigations, contribute to accountability at all levels.

MOTOR OIL Group's policy applies to all employees, regardless of contractual status and extends to trainees, apprentices, volunteers and third-party collaborators. The Group's Human Resources Manager,

as the designated Reference Person, holds high accountability in guiding employees on prevention and response mechanisms. Transparency is key - policy details are accessible through its intranet, informational materials and direct communication channels, ensuring that all stakeholders remain informed and protected.

Employees who report concerns through internal channels are safeguarded against retaliation. This protection extends not only to them but also to their colleagues, relatives and external contractors, reinforcing a culture, where speaking up is encouraged and met with support.

MOTOR OIL Group recognizes its responsibility to create an environment where fairness, equity and respect are non-negotiable. By integrating human rights principles into Group's daily operations, it continues to shape a workplace that values dignity and ensures a future built on ethical business practices.

<!-- Report Page 222 -->
##### Training & Development

MOTOR OIL Group recognizes that continuous learning and development are critical to maintaining a competitive and skilled workforce. The Learning & Development Policy is designed to enhance employees' knowledge, skills and career progression opportunities through educational initiatives. It ensures that employees at all levels have access to training programs tailored to business needs and individual career aspirations. The policy is applicable to all employees across the Group, except for temporary workers, interns, external contractors and refinery employees, who are covered under a separate Refinery-Specific Training Policy.

To facilitate professional growth, the Group follows the 70-20-10 learning model, which balances experiential learning (70%), social learning (20%) and formal training (10%). This model ensures that employees gain practical experience on the job while also benefiting from learning opportunities and mentorship. Talent Management & Acquisition Division is responsible for overseeing the implementation and effectiveness of these policies, ensuring alignment with the Group's strategic priorities.

Comprehensive training programs at MOTOR OIL Group are designed to support both business needs and employee career progression. Regulatory compliance remains a priority, with mandatory training covering GDPR, cybersecurity, health and safety and corporate ethics, ensuring alignment with national and international labor standards. At the same time, job-specific expertise is strengthened through technical and functional training, particularly in operational roles such as refinery work, engineering and energy management. Recognizing that professional growth extends beyond technical knowledge, the Group places strong emphasis on soft skills, incorporating leadership, communication, teamwork and emotional intelligence development. Employees also benefit from access to professional qualification programs and industry-recognized certifications.

MOTOR OIL Group's training initiatives are delivered through a variety of learning formats, including inperson sessions, e-Learning and blended learning approaches. Employees engage in global learning platforms, industry conferences and postgraduate scholarship programs designed to foster long-term professional development. Structured mentorship and leadership development initiatives play a critical role in career advancement, particularly for underrepresented groups, reinforcing its commitment to workforce diversity and inclusion.

Training is scheduled based on workforce planning needs, closely monitored through SAP SuccessFactors and adaptable to individual requests. Employees can submit ad-hoc training requests, which are reviewed in collaboration with direct managers and HR, ensuring strategic alignment and resource efficiency. Engineers and white-collar staff follow an open and transparent training request procedure, while a structured funding process supports master's degree programs. In 2025, MOTOR OIL Group will introduce an Individual Development Plan pilot for engineers, focusing on personalized career growth and targeted upskilling.

A Workforce Skills Continuity framework tracks employee competencies per job code, aligning training schedules with shift programs to minimize disruption while accommodating additional learning opportunities. Internship programs further support early-career development, providing young professionals with valuable industry exposure.

International Assignments expand cultural awareness and professional exposure, allowing employees to engage in cross-location exchanges. At MOTOR OIL Group, its people are supported throughout their professional journey by offering a wide range of upskilling, reskilling programs. To further support continuous learning, the Group have introduced the "My Learning Path" initiative, leveraging SAP SuccessFactors as its core Learning Management System (LMS). This system provides access to over 9,000 courses from leading global training institutions, created to enhance employee development through a robust approach. The rollout includes: (1) building awareness through continuous learning reminders, (2) contextualizing learning based on business needs such as Finance, Sales and Performance Management, (3) creating a learner-driven culture where employees take ownership of their personal development within MOTOR OIL Group's strategic framework. Through Group's Leadership Development programs, its aim to cultivate, visionary leaders by equipping them with essential skills and an engaging leadership mindset. This empowers them to inspire their teams and drive long-term success. Group have established development centers designed to cultivate skills and support employee growth and career progression.

To ensure sustainable leadership pipelines, the Group has established the MOTOR OIL Mentoring Program, a development initiative aimed at enhancing learning agility and supporting employee growth. Additionally, the Talent Assessment and Selection Workshop equips leaders with talent selection competencies, ensuring fair and competency-based hiring decisions.

Training effectiveness is closely tracked through participation rates, skill development metrics and posttraining assessments. SAP SuccessFactors documents individual development plans, ensuring alignment between training efforts, employee career trajectories and organizational needs. Employees complete designated training modules within specified timeframes, while feedback mechanisms, satisfaction surveys and post-training evaluations help refine programs to maintain alignment with both business objectives and employee aspirations.

<!-- Report Page 224 -->
##### Refinery activities

Employees follow a specialized Refinery Training Policy designed to uphold high operational and safety standards. Process safety, hazardous material management, emergency response and regulatory compliance are integral to refinery training modules. Certification programs include Permit-to-Work authorization, fire safety procedures and advanced engineering skills. Periodic retraining ensures employees maintain up-to-date knowledge, covering startup/shutdown procedures, environmental compliance and risk management protocols. Compliance with ISO-18788, ISO-27001 and emergency response plans is reinforced through recertification programs developed in coordination with regulatory bodies and industry experts.

Training activities within the refinery are managed collaboratively by the Training and Development Department and refinery departments. Each department appoints an "Area Training Leader" responsible for overseeing training initiatives, as detailed in their job description. A "Skills Map" is developed for each job position, specifying mandatory training subjects, reference materials, course descriptions, trainer lists, delivery methods (classroom, e-learning, field demonstration, simulator exercises, off-site training) and assessment criteria.

Scheduled retraining is required at a minimum in key areas, including:. Fire safety and startup/shutdown emergency procedures for production units.. Work Permit Certification: every three years for issuers, recipients and inspectors; every five years or approvers.. Alkylation unit entry certification every three years.. Fire safety training as per DEP HSM-36.

· ISO-18788 and ISO-27001 requirements for all refinery personnel and security teams.. Emergency response planning for the Duty Engineer.. Facility Contingency Plan for the jetty.

· ISO 17025 requirements for laboratory operations.. First aid training for rescuers and nurses every three years.. IRATA rescuer certifications per industry requirements.. Inspection certifications.. Professional certifications (Welder, Boiler Technician, Heavy Equipment Operator) as per their expiration.

Through these structured and evolving training programs, MOTOR OIL Group supports its employees to remain at the forefront of industry developments while fostering a culture of continuous learning, operational excellence and workplace safety.

##### Talent and Performance Management

The Talent Management & Acquisition Policy supports a meritocratic and transparent recruitment process, emphasizing equal opportunity hiring. Hiring decisions are based solely on qualifications, skills and experience, eliminating bias and fostering an inclusive workforce. This policy ensures that candidates are assessed fairly, with recruitment teams trained on non-discriminatory hiring practices. The General Manager of Human Resources oversees its implementation, ensuring compliance with national labor laws and internal workforce planning strategies.

The Performance Management and Employee Development Policy establishes a career progression framework, ensuring that employees receive clear performance evaluations, feedback and opportunities for professional growth. Additionally, one of Performance Management and Employee Development Policy is to strengthen the Group's commitment to the principles and values that govern ESG (Environment, Society, Governance) through key performance indicators, as well as to promote personal responsibility regarding its impact on society and the environment. Through SAP SuccessFactors, Group tracks performance metrics, aligning individual career development with organizational goals. Employees are provided with structured career pathways and have access to internal mobility programs through the Internal Career Opportunities Policy, ensuring career advancement and reskilling opportunities across different business units.

<!-- Report Page 226 -->
##### 8.2.2 Policies and Actions related to Working Conditions [S1-1] [S1-4]

MOTOR OIL Group is committed to promote an inclusive, fair and equitable working environment that upholds internationally recognized labor standards and human rights. Group's policies and actions support diversity, non-discrimination, fair compensation, health and safety and professional development, aligned to internationally recognized frameworks, as incorporated into Greek legislation, such as ILO Declaration on Fundamental Principles and Rights at Work.

To effectively manage material impacts, risks and opportunities related to its workforce, the Group has implemented policies that promote occupational health and safety, fair treatment, social dialogue and continuous development. The General Manager of Human Resources oversees these policies, ensuring regular assessments and compliance with international labor standards.

The Group ensures that workforce policies are developed with input from HR professionals, senior executives, DEI specialists and employees. Regular employee feedback surveys contribute to refining policies related to equal opportunities, fairness in career progression and professional development. Additionally, external engagement with professional organizations and educational institutions supports its commitment to fostering an inclusive workplace.

To promote transparency, all policies are accessible through the Group's intranet, ensuring employees are aware of their rights and responsibilities. Employees also receive policy training during onboarding, including modules on ethics, corporate responsibility and whistleblowing.

##### Health & Safety

To navigate workforce-related risks, opportunities and material impacts, policies foster occupational health and safety, fair treatment, social dialogue and continuous development. Oversight by the General Manager of Human Resources ensures compliance with international labor standards, with regular assessments refining Group's approach.

Developing workforce policies involves collaboration with HR professionals, senior executives, DEI specialists and employees. Continuous feedback through surveys sharpens policies on equal opportunities, career progression and professional growth. Engaging externally with professional organizations and educational institutions further strengthens Group's inclusive workplace commitment.

Transparency remains fundamental. Employees receive training on ethics, corporate responsibility and whistleblowing as part of their onboarding.

To fulfil MOTOR OIL Group's commitment for a safe working environment, MOTOR OIL Group has developed and implemented an Integrated Management System covering areas such as Quality, Health, Safety, Security and Environment (QHSSE Management System) applied to all employees and workers who are not employees such as contractors, security personnels, interns across every operation. The QHSSE Management System is a line management accountability system and an integral part of how the Group conducts business. Its aim is to continuously improve processes and performance by assessing risks and controlling those as well as builds up from potential incidents identification and control (not just on actual incidents or near misses).

MOTOR OIL Group's Health and Safety Policy based on the "Goal Zero" philosophy, prioritizes accident prevention, occupational hazard elimination and operational integrity. Seeking zero harm, zero fatalities and zero significant incidents, the policy is embedded in an Integrated Management System (QHSSE), covering Quality, Health, Safety, Security and Environment. Compliance with ISO 45001:2018 ensures adherence to global occupational health and safety standards, while proactive risk assessments and hazard prevention strategies safeguard employees, contractors and operations.

MOTOR OIL Group's commitment extends beyond its workforce, to contractors, interns, security personnel and refinery visitors. Safety instructions upon entry reinforce expectations. Senior and line management

bear accountability for policy implementation, ensuring continuous improvements.

Certified under ISO 45001:2018, the major operational sites' safety framework aligns with the European Directive 2012/18/EU (SEVESO III) and national legislation regarding hazardous circumstances. Protecting employees, contractors, local communities and business partners, the MOTOR OIL Group fosters open communication, promotes active safety participation and provides targeted training. The integration of environmental protection strategies ensures resilience in operations, benefiting both internal and external stakeholders.

Emergency preparedness remains a priority, with identified scenarios forming the basis of regularly tested response plans. All incidents, from near misses to significant events, undergo thorough investigation and documentation. Senior management continuously reviews and updates policies to maintain alignment with industry standards and best practices.

###### Retail activities

The Group extends its commitment to safety, social responsibility and labor standards into the retail sector, according to the following policies.

##### Safety & Security Policy

Safeguarding people, assets, information and corporate reputation, systematically addresses security matters. National laws, international standards and the Group's General Business Principles guide security management, with supervisors and specialized security firms overseeing implementation. Annual risk assessments inform proactive security measures, ensuring compliance while reinforcing a culture of vigilance.

##### Driving Policy

Recognizing driving as a high-risk activity, Retail Companies enforce strict road safety measures. Road Safety Studies guide hazard identification, regulatory compliance underpins operational standards and performance monitoring supports accident prevention. Comprehensive training for employees and contractors ensures alignment with the highest safety standards.

##### Drugs and Alcohol Policy

Maintaining a workplace free from substance-related impairment is a fundamental responsibility. Retail strictly prohibits the use, possession, or distribution of drugs and alcohol within its operations. Employees in high-risk roles, particularly in fuel, lubricant and chemical handling, undergo routine and specialized testing. Medical examinations post-incident further upholds workplace safety while respecting confidentiality.

##### Product Stewardship Policy

An approach to product management, ensuring compliance with regulatory standards while mitigating risks to employees, contractors, customers and the environment. Safety Data Sheets (MSDS) facilitate informed handling, storage and transport and emergency response plans safeguard against hazardous material incidents. Collaboration with experts and regulatory bodies drives continuous improvements in safety and environmental responsibility.

##### Non-Smoking Policy

Retail Companies reinforce health-focused workplace policies by regulating smoking in designated outdoor areas. Employees and visitors share responsibility for compliance, ensuring a work environment that prioritizes well-being.

<!-- Report Page 228 -->
##### Major Accident Prevention Policy

Managing risks inherent in petroleum and chemical product storage, handling and transport, retail companies adhere to SEVESO III regulations and national laws. The Major Accident Prevention Policy upholds stakeholder safety, extending protection to employees, contractors, visitors and local communities.

###### Refinery activities

All necessary measures are implemented to prevent accidents and injuries occurring in the workplace, aiming to minimize, the risks associated with the work environment. This includes a thorough consideration of the specific risks inherent in Group's activities. In the refinery, it is ensured that all staff receive effective environmental, safety and hygiene instructions on a regular basis. Health and safety and environmental training is repeated for new and relocated staff as well as learning from Incidents is a process which employees and the organization as a whole seek to understand any negative safety events that have taken place to prevent similar future events. In addition, it provides the personnel with the necessary and appropriate personal protective equipment, certified for protecting the employee for the specific job. In the event of a work-related accident, the company has adequate facilities (first aid rooms) to provide first aid and ensures that the employee receives further medical attention.

As part of its interest in employee welfare, Group's personnel and their families are provided a private life insurance and medical care program covering all hospital treatment expenses that may arise. The company also has a multi-membered safety section and specialized safety officers who are assigned the responsibility to identify the hazards, to recommend the specific preventive measures for each activity, to supervise and secure health and safety conditions at workplace as well as to reinforce the safety culture through safety walkthrough and meetings. As an aid to workplace accident prevention, the Company owns fire extinction vehicles and a multitude of stable and portable fire & smoke detection systems and extinguishers.

The accident prevention policy is implemented through the following methods,

· Strict adherence to legislation and internationally accepted codes, protocols and safety operation rules. Continuous improvement of all safety and health control systems. Record keeping of all incidents and near misses, investigations and adopting appropriate corrective action. Continuous upgrade of all resources relating to individual safety and fire prevention, combined with personnel training on the use of these resources

The operational framework is considered, both internal and external factors, legislative requirements and stakeholder expectations, identifying the threats and opportunities that must be addressed including health & safety requirements and possible emergencies.

The Group addresses risks and opportunities in order, to ensure that the Integrated Management System can achieve the desired results, to enhance the desired effects, to eliminate or reduce unwanted effects, including the possibility of company's affecting from external environmental conditions and to achieve performance improvement.

Identifying risks and opportunities, as well as analysing, assessing and addressing risks, is an integral process within the company, carried out by upper management and executives in accordance with the Risk Management Procedure. This process is supported by daily meetings focused on business planning and operational procedures. The Integrated Management System Section is responsible for documentation, while risk management actions are embedded within the Integrated Management System processes.

The company takes actions to deal with threats and seize opportunities, address legal and other requirements and prepare and respond to emergencies. These actions are integrated into the processes of the Integrated Management System and the other business processes of the company and their effectiveness is evaluated.

In planning actions, the company takes into consideration,. the hierarchy of controls and the results of the Integrated Management System. best practical technological choices and

· financial, operational and company requirements.

The H&S objectives and targets - as they result from the applied risk analysis methods - can be achieved either, by their incorporation in the Occupational Risk Assessment or through specific H&S management programs. Occupational Risk Assessment methodology, as well as an H&S program, include and describe the necessary actions for achieving objectives and targets, means and time frame by which they are to be achieved and the relevant authorities and responsibilities for the above actions.

MOTOR OIL Group has established, implements and maintains processes for the communication and participation of employees of all levels and functions and, where appropriate, of employee representatives in the development, planning, implementation, performance evaluation and actions, improving the Occupational Health and Safety (OHS) management system. Participation allows employees to be included in the decision-making processes regarding Occupational Health and Safety (OHS) performance measures and proposed changes.

MOTOR OIL Group ensures that employees at all levels are encouraged to report hazardous situations so that preventive measures can be taken and corrective actions be implemented.

Specifically, the company has the mechanisms, time, training and resources necessary for consultation and participation. In these mechanisms is included the possibility of representing employees through the Committee on Occupational Health and Safety (EYAE - joint meetings of EYAE with the management of the Refinery to promote hygiene and safety issues in the workplace). It also provides timely access to clear, comprehensible and relevant information on the Health and Safety Management System which is developed in accordance with the requirements of the ISO 45001:2018 standard. Additionally, the company proactively identifies and eliminates barriers that may hinder employee participation, such as failure to address employee input or suggestions, language and literacy challenges, retaliation or the threat of retaliation and policies or practices that discourage or suppress employee engagement. Lastly, it emphasizes the consultation and active participation of all staff, including employees who do not hold managerial positions.

###### Circular economy & alternative fuels

Advancing sustainability initiatives, THALIS prioritizes safety and regulatory compliance, fostering a culture of continuous improvement through audits, risk assessments and training. Industry-specific strategies align with best practices, ensuring resilience in an evolving regulatory landscape.

In addition, VERD & PRASINO LADI integrate a structured and proactive approach to health and safety, ensuring compliance with legal frameworks while fostering a culture of preparedness and risk awareness. The adherence to national and industry-specific regulations, including P.D. 17/1996 and Law 3850/10, establishes a foundation where employee protection, operational security and environmental responsibility are prioritized. By implementing a comprehensive Internal Emergency Plan, the company ensures that all potential risks, from fire incidents to chemical leaks, extreme weather conditions, seismic events and security threats, are systematically assessed and addressed through pre-established protocols.



Emergency preparedness is reinforced through clearly defined response mechanisms, minimizing reaction time and human error during critical situations. The development of action plans for various scenarios, such as chemical leaks in storage or production areas, ensures that containment measures are effectively implemented, mitigating potential hazards to both employees and the surrounding environment. Preventative measures are further strengthened through the identification of evacuation routes, prioritization of areas requiring immediate action and coordination among designated response teams, aiming for a seamless and efficient reaction in times of crisis.

A strong emphasis is placed on training and continuous education, equipping employees with the necessary knowledge to act decisively and systematically. By fostering a workplace culture centered on safety, employees are empowered to recognize potential threats, respond appropriately and contribute to a collective effort in maintaining a secure environment. The integration of communication systems across all levels of operation enhances coordination, allowing for swift decision-making and immediate notification of authorities or neighboring facilities when necessary.

Beyond immediate emergency response, VERD & PRASINO LADI ensures the long-term protection of its workforce, infrastructure and surrounding community by continuously refining safety procedures and adapting to evolving risks. A structured approach to risk management translates into a safer workplace, reduced operational disruptions and strengthened trust among employees, partners and regulatory bodies.

<!-- Report Page 230 -->
##### Secure employment

MOTOR OIL Group fosters a work environment where opportunities, work-life balance and social protection are fundamental to employee well-being. A framework of policies (e.g. Rewards & Recognition Policy, Talent Acquisition & Recruitment) ensures transparency in hiring, equitable compensation and career development, reinforcing both national regulations and international labor standards. Fairness is embedded in every aspect of employment, from structured salary frameworks to recognition programs that celebrate contributions aligned with corporate values (Rewards & Recognition Policy).

The Group believes in meritocracy, where compensation reflects job responsibilities, experience and industry benchmarks. A grade-based salary structure eliminates disparities, ensuring remuneration remains competitive while maintaining internal equity. Performance-based incentives, allocated transparently through MOTOR OIL Group's Rewards & Recognition Policy, strengthen fairness across all business units. Beyond financial rewards, acknowledgment of employee contributions nurtures a workplace culture driven by motivation and excellence.

The Group has adopted an equal pay across without discrimination and regardless of gender, race, or any other factors, reflecting its continuous dedication to fairness and respect. By upholding this principle, not only the Group promotes equity and commitment to fostering a fair workplace but also its employees feel valued and appreciated for their contributions. Regular salary audits and equity reviews maintain alignment with market standards and internal principles, reinforcing a system where recognition is earned and fairness is given.

##### Work-life balance

At MOTOR OIL Group the importance of family and personal well-being is recognized. Along with the regulatory respective family-related leave options (e.g. paternity leave, parental leave, carer's leave, force majeure leaves and flexible working arrangements for parents and carers) the Group additionally provides a wide range of benefits (eg. Childcare support), in order, to support its team members during significant life events.

MOTOR OIL Group 'Rewards and Recognition Policy' has been conducted to celebrate the achievements and contributions of its employees, ensuring that their hard work and dedication are acknowledged.

By placing equal emphasis on both professional achievement and personal well-being, Group is dedicated to cultivating a supportive and balanced environment where its employees can excel in both their careers and personal lives. The Group offers a comprehensive range of employee benefits, including insurance policies that provide pension and medical treatment coverage, educational assistance schemes, Christmas parties for employees' families, long service awards, support for employees involved in sporting activities and discounts. These benefits are available to all employees based on their position and level within the organization, rather than the nature of their employment contract.

The Group remains committed to fostering a culture where productivity thrives alongside personal goals and development. Programs that safeguard physical and mental well-being complement these practices, ensuring employees feel supported at every stage of their professional and personal journeys.

##### Social Protection and Income Security

MOTOR OIL Group prioritizes efforts to safeguard its employees' financial security against challenges such as retirement, long-term illness, disability, or unemployment. To achieve this, the Group adheres to national labor laws and corporate agreements, providing severance payments and retirement benefits as required. Also, the Group offers a competitive compensation that reflects its employees' skills, experience and dedication. This approach underscores its commitment to attracting and retaining top talent, while fostering a positive and rewarding work environment for everyone within the organization and ensuring compliance with the minimum wage standards mandated by Greek law.

MOTOR OIL Group's commitment extends to comprehensive healthcare coverage for all employees, with public healthcare provided as part of the statutory benefits. For those with indefinite-term contracts, private health insurance through Generali enhances their coverage, offering protection against income loss due to illness or accident. This forms part of a broader strategy to secure its employees' well-being, which includes income protection from the start of their employment. In addition, for employees who voluntarily left are covered by social protection through Greek Law, provided that they must have completed 80 days of work in each of the two previous years before receiving benefits.

In the event of long-term illness or disability, both EFKA (National Social Security Fund) and private insurance schemes play a critical role in offering continued financial support, ensuring income security for affected employees. As part of its ongoing commitment to inclusion, the Group works diligently to foster an environment that supports employees with disabilities. By promoting workplace accessibility, Group ensures that everyone, regardless of their circumstances, has equal opportunities for growth and advancement within the company.

Tracking the effectiveness of its policies and actions related to material sustainability impacts, risks and opportunities, the Group is committed to adhering strictly to relevant targets and the scope of its policies. The Group monitors and evaluates relevant metrics on a case-by-case basis. Key metrics include those related to Diversity, Training and Skills Development and Health & Safety (can be found, under the Metrics segment). Additional metrics may also cover turnover ratios, the proportion of women in the total workforce and the percentage of women in management positions.

With regards to the achieved positive outcomes of its actions, in 2024, the Group's personnel completed 123,363 training hours on various topics, including 'Health & Safety' and 'Whistleblowing wrongdoings6' at work. The effectiveness of these actions is reflected in metrics such as Health and Safety incidents, complaints and severe human rights impacts.

" The Whistleblowing Wrongdoings are the following: The violations of EU law within the meaning of Article 4 of Law no. 4990/2022, as currently in force, as well as the legislation on combating violence and harassment at work, within the meaning of Articles 3 and 4 of Law no. 4808/2021. In addition, violations of the provisions of the Anti-Bribery and Corruption Policy and of the Group's Code of Conduct and Corporate Responsibility also fall within the scope of Wrongdoings.

Additionally, the outcomes are assessed through a biennial Employee Survey, with the most recent survey conducted in 2023. Survey results showed that,. 76% of employees agree the Group strictly adheres to Health & Safety measures.. 59% agree the Group provides sufficient job training.. 59% agree the Group has adequate performance management processes.
