##### 8.2.3 Processes for Engaging with Own Workforce and Workers' Representatives about Impacts [S1-2]

MOTOR OIL Group prioritizes employee engagement and continuous improvement of workforce policies through feedback mechanisms. Employees participate in satisfaction surveys, engagement forums and direct reporting channels, ensuring that workplace policies remain aligned with employee needs and evolving labor market trends.

To reinforce transparency, all workforce policies are accessible through digital platforms, ensuring employees are fully informed of their rights, benefits and professional development opportunities. Additionally, onboarding training includes mandatory policy briefings, ensuring that employees are well-versed in workplace rights and corporate governance policies.

Through satisfaction surveys, employee sentiment is monitored, allowing us to make data-driven improvements to workforce policies. Dedicated communication channels, ensuring open, twoway communication through focus groups, leadership feedback sessions and structured reporting mechanisms. Employees are encouraged to provide constructive feedback on career progression, diversity and work-life balance, ensuring continuous refinement of employment practices. The engagement through satisfaction surveys is conducted bi-annually. Ther is no pre-defined frequency on the leadership feedback on the results of the survey. By analyzing the results, the Group is committed to work on optimized processes, organization and decision-making methods.

The Group engages both directly with its workforce and with workers' representatives. Engagement occurs regularly through various mechanisms, including performance evaluations, feedback meetings, union meetings, regular joint meetings between Health & Safety Committee and management. Also, continuous monitoring and control of maintenance work with meetings held with contractors, weekly safety reports and monthly reports with critical safety indicators. Additionally, Safety Day events and weekly fire safety drills and Time Out for Safety (TOS) meetings are taking into place in order, to address safety observations and conditions. Refinery employees are represented through their trade union and the Health and Safety Committee. Specifically for the Health and Safety issues, corrective actions are considered a very important function of the Integrated Management System. In the refinery, working teams of relevant personnel are formed to analyze the Health & Safety issues and identify the required corrective action, which is approved by the management prior to its implementation. Records of corrective actions taken are maintained by the relevant Departments.

The Whistleblower Investigation Officer (W.I.O.) is responsible for receiving whistleblower disclosures and overseeing the investigation process. The W.I.O. operates independently and objectively and reports to the Board of Directors. The Reports Management Committee (R.M.C.) supports the W.I.O. and also reports to the Board of Directors. Additionally, the Compliance Officer or the Officer Responsible for Risk Management and Reporting (ORRMR) ensures engagement mechanisms are in place. Specifically, the OMRR, is responsible for designing and coordinating training activities on ethics and integrity while it participates in the development of internal policies to enhance integrity and transparency in the Group. Ultimately, the Board of Directors, having been informed on the progress of results of the investigations by the RMC and particularly the ORRMR, takes decisions on taking any corrective actions.

MOTOR OIL Group complies with Law 4990/2022, which transposed EU Directive 2019/1937 into Greek law, protecting persons who report breaches of EU law. The Collective Bargaining Agreement with the Hellenic Federation of Enterprises describes the terms of employment and pay levels at the refinery, aiming to achieve continuous improvements in working conditions and develop mutually beneficial relations. Furthermore, within the code of ethics and corporate responsibility, which applies to all employees of the group, is specifically mentioned the respect and the support for human rights.

The Group, through its sphere of influence, supports and respects the protection of human rights and ensures that any type of violation or infringement thereof is avoided. Inter alia, the avoidance of child and forced labor and the respect for the principles of non-discrimination in the workplace.

MOTOR OIL Group assesses the effectiveness of engagement through continuous monitoring and control of maintenance work, monthly and weekly reports with critical safety indicators, safety management assessment programs, training programs and feedback mechanisms, employee performance management systems including annual performance evaluations and employee engagement surveys such as the updated survey launched in 2023.

The outcomes of the engagement of own workforce, is communicated internally. For example, the results the employee survey was communicated through intranet to all the employees. The resources allocated to engagement, are among others, the HR General Division and the Group's Health and Safety departments along with other departments within the organization that they might be involved in the impacts.

MOTOR OL Group has adopted a Whistleblowing Policy that protects employees against retaliation and ensures the protection of personal data for all parties involved. The Local Safety Committee represents all workers and contractors and discusses health and safety hazards, ensuring strong participation from various roles such as QHSSE Advisors, Technical Inspectors, Occupational Hygienists and a Safety Technician.

Lastly, MOTOR OIL Group has adopted a comprehensive and extent Stakeholder Engagement Plan (SEP), which aims to enhance transparency, strengthen two-way communication and align MOTOR OIL with social, environmental and regulatory expectations. It includes analyzing the connection between each stakeholder group and the company to identify critical interaction points, understand impacts and interdependencies and recognize potential conflicts. Additionally, the plan ensures compliance with ESG standards while identifying risks and opportunities that arise from stakeholder relationships, fostering stable and effective long-term partnerships.

##### 8.2.4 Processes to Remediate Negative Impacts and Channels for Own Workforce to Raise Concerns [S1-3]

The Group has recognised a negative impact on Health and Safety. In response to that, it has Upholding ethical labor standards that require more than policies; it demands a culture where integrity is protected and concerns are addressed. MOTOR OIL Group recognizes that ensuring accountability strengthens trust and corporate governance. A key component of this commitment is the Whistleblowing Policy, a framework that guarantees employees and stakeholders access to confidential and anonymous reporting channels, reinforcing transparency at every level. This policy is designed to align with Greek legal frameworks, notably Law no. 4990/2022 and Law no. 4808/2021, ensuring that all reports are handled with diligence and fairness. The Group's Reports Management Committee (R.M.C.) systematically reviews cases, applying corrective measures where necessary. Employees benefit from multiple reporting options, including an electronic platform, written submissions and direct meetings with the Whistleblower Investigation Officer (W.I.O.) or Compliance Officer, each ensuring confidentiality.

The Group has established multiple reporting channels. Any employee, external partner or other person falling within the scope of the Whistleblowing Policy as well as the Policy against Violence and Harassment at Work suspecting that a wrongdoing (as mentioned at the above Policies) is occurring may contact the Whistleblower Investigation Officer (W.I.O.) or the Compliance Officer (for the Group companies that have not designated an W.I.O.) and in any case to communicate by name or anonymously through the following communication channels:

· Whistleblowing Platform: whistleblowing.moh.gr. Written report - Letter marked «attention of the W.I.O. or of the Compliance Officer (for Group companies that have not appointed an W.I.O.) >> or «Reference of Law 4808/2021 or Law 4990/2022» to the address: Irodou Attikou 12A, 151 24, Marousi, Greece. Personal meeting with the W.I.O. or with the Compliance Officer (for Group companies that have not appointed a W.I.O.), upon submission of a request in writing or orally or via email to the W.I.O. or the Compliance Officer respectively. Furthermore, in the event that a whistleblowing report is received by an unauthorized person, they are obliged to forward it to the W.I.O. immediately, without any modification of its content or disclosure of information that may lead to the identification of the whistleblower, or any third party named in the report, in compliance with the provisions of Law 4990/2022.

Whistleblowing is not confined to internal misconduct; it extends to external risks that may compromise business ethics. A grievance mechanism is in place to address fraud, corruption, discrimination, workplace violence and other regulatory breaches. This applies not only to employees but also to external partners and individuals connected to the Group, ensuring that ethical expectations are upheld across all stakeholder interactions.

MOTOR OIL Group prioritizes an environment where reporting misconduct does not result in retaliation. Exclusion from professional development programs, negative performance evaluations, or workplace ostracization are actively prevented. Awareness campaigns, internal communications and training programs reinforce this protection, ensuring that employees understand their rights and the mechanisms available to them. Beyond traditional reporting avenues, the Group has embedded additional safeguards within its broader governance framework. The Committee on Occupational Health and Safety (EYAE) functions as a collaborative forum where employees and management jointly address workplace hygiene and safety concerns. These discussions further enhance transparency while identifying risks before they escalate into critical issues. In the context of the Integrated Management System, corrective actions are considered a very important. In the refinery, a working team of relevant personnel is formed in order to analyze the Health & Safety issue and identify the required corrective action, which is approved by the management prior to its implementation. Records of corrective actions taken are maintained by the relevant Departments.

Ensuring these channels are trusted requires more than policy enforcement - it demands continuous engagement. MOTOR OIL Group integrates whistleblowing awareness into broader corporate communications, leveraging company's intranet (SharePoint), newsletters and email updates to keep employees informed. Training sessions provide practical guidance, reinforcing the message that raising concerns is not just permitted but encouraged. The Group employs structured monitoring through control mechanisms such as safety management assessments, maintenance work reviews and key safety indicator reports. Monthly and weekly updates track trends, allowing for targeted improvements in response protocols and risk mitigation strategies.

Transparency is also reinforced through MOTOR OIL Group's commitment to structured dialogue. The Local Safety Committee, which includes QHSSE Advisors, Technical Inspectors, Occupational Hygienists and Safety Technicians, convenes regularly to evaluate emerging concerns.

At the core of these efforts is a firm stance against retaliation. The Whistleblowing Policy explicitly safeguards individuals who use reporting channels, from any form of adverse action. Protection measures extend beyond formal policies, embedding anti-retaliation principles into the organization's ethical framework to uphold fairness and trust. Ultimately, integrity and accountability are not static objectives but ongoing commitments. By maintaining rigorous oversight and continuously refining whistleblowing mechanisms, the Group ensures that its governance structures remain robust. MOTOR OIL Group believes that ethical operations do not merely protect employees - they enhance long-term resilience, investor confidence and corporate sustainability.

<!-- Report Page 234 -->
##### 8.2.5 Targets related to Health & Safety [S1-5]

MOTOR OIL Group prioritizes a safe work environment, integrating rigorous Health & Safety measures designed for accident prevention and risk mitigation. These initiatives are supported by strict adherence to industry regulations, ongoing monitoring and structured programs aimed at reinforcing employee well-being and operational resilience. Central to these efforts is the Group's ongoing "Goal Zero" target, a firm commitment to achieving zero fatalities, preventing major industrial accidents and reducing workplace incidents across all operations, including refining, marketing and distribution.

With absolute targets for zero fatalities and major industrial accidents, the Group also strives to position its Lost Workday Incident Frequency (LWIF) and Process Safety Event Rate (PSER) within the second quartile of European industry benchmarks.

The Group aligns its safety measures with global standards such as ISO 45001 certification and European Directive 2012/18/EU on major accident prevention. Scientific principles and risk assessments guide target-setting, while industry benchmarking and feedback from stakeholders - including management, safety committees, workforce representatives and external auditors - ensure that these goals are both ambitious and achievable. Stakeholder engagement plays a critical role in maintaining alignment with evolving safety priorities and regulatory requirements.

Performance is monitored through monthly tracking of incident rates and safety compliance, ensuring that progress is continuously assessed. Employee feedback on safety training helps refine and enhance the programs, supporting the Group's commitment to a resilient and responsive safety culture. Targets are regularly updated, incorporating changes in regulatory frameworks, industry benchmarks and incident analysis to maintain the highest standards.

At present, MOTOR OIL Group has not established specific targets related to violence and harassment. However, the effectiveness of its active policy is demonstrated by the absence of any related incidents within the Group.

##### 8.2.6 Targets related to Equal Treatment and Opportunities for All [S1-5] ]

At MOTOR OIL Group, while there is not specific setting targets related to equal treatment and opportunity, its commitment to these principles is deeply ingrained in the organizational culture. The Group continuously track and assess the effectiveness of its policies and actions, as previously mentioned in section 'Equal treatment and opportunities for all | Policies and actions [S1-1] [S1-4]' through regular evaluations and feedback mechanisms, ensuring that its practices promote equality and diversity. By focusing on measurable outcomes and maintaining an open dialogue with its employees, the Group strive to create a work environment where every individual has the opportunity, to succeed and thrive. In future reporting periods, Group intends to examine establishing specific targets related to the topic.

###### 8.3.6 Social protection [S1-11]

The topic of 'social protection' has not been classified as material based on the DMA outcomes. However, in keeping with MOTOR OIL Group commitment to transparency and responsible reporting, it continues to disclose relevant metrics on this matter. MOTOR OIL Group ensures compliance with EU regulations and national social protection frameworks, with its primary operations in Greece. Firstly, sickness benefits are fully covered under Greek legislation and EU regulations. Employees in Greece are protected by the EFKA (National Social Security Fund), which provides medical expenses, hospitalization and sick leave benefits. Additionally, Regulation (EC) No 883/2004 ensures continuity of healthcare coverage for employees working across EU member states, while Directive 2011/24/EU guarantees access to medical treatment in other EU countries under comparable conditions. In addition, unemployment benefits are partially covered, with EU regulations coordinating benefits while Greek law determines eligibility and amounts. Employees in Greece are entitled to unemployment support through OAED (Greek Employment Organization), which administers benefits according to national legislation.

Moreover, employment injury and acquired disability coverage are fully provided under both Greek law and EU regulations. Workers in Greece are insured through EFKA (National Social Security Fund), ensuring compensation for workplace injuries and acquired disabilities. Regulation (EC) No 883/2004 facilitates the coordination of such benefits for employees moving between EU states. At the same time, Directive 89/391/EEC mandates occupational safety and health measures to prevent workplace risks.

Equally important, parental leave rights are fully secured through Directive 2019/1158/EU, which has been transposed into Greek labor law. In Greece, mothers receive paid maternity leave for 17 weeks, while fathers are entitled to 14 days of paid paternity leave. Furthermore, employees can extend their leave through unpaid parental leave or special childcare leave, partially compensated through social security schemes. Also, the Group provides severance payments and retirement benefits as required, by EU regulations ensuring the portability of pension rights while Greek law defines the benefit structure. Employees contribute to EFKA (National Social Security Fund), which administers both national and supplementary pension schemes.

#### 9 AFFECTED COMMUNITIES [S3]

##### 9.1 Strategy

###### 9.1.1 Material Impacts, Risks and Opportunities and their interaction with Strategy and Business Model [S3.SBM-3]

The primary beneficiaries of the Group's initiatives are local communities - meaning communities that are either directly engaged with or located around the facilities, as well as the broader national context in which the core operations of the organization are conducted - which experience significant improvements in infrastructure, access to essential services and overall quality of life. Through the Group's investments, residents benefit from enhanced economic opportunities, better healthcare facilities, improved transportation networks and the creation of job opportunities, leading to increased employment prospects and community well-being. These benefits are particularly impactful for vulnerable groups within local communities, such as low-income families, youth and the elderly, who stand to gain from improved social services and economic opportunities.

Although MOTOR OIL Group has not identified any significant impacts on affected communities, the Group remains committed to supporting these communities, particularly those located near its areas of operation.

These communities are often at the heart of the Group's engagement efforts, as the development and operational activities surrounding the refinery play an important role in shaping their daily lives. By improving essential infrastructure and promoting social development, the Group's investments help uplift these communities, making them more resilient and better equipped for long-term prosperity.

##### Risks and opportunities

The operation of refineries, renewable energy installations and petroleum facilities introduces significant risks and opportunities for MOTOR OIL Group, with direct financial implications tied to the impacts on local communities and stakeholders. Noise, visual disturbances and landscape changes can affect the quality of life for nearby residents, potentially leading to dissatisfaction, reduced property values and increased regulatory scrutiny. These risks can result in increased costs related to environmental protection, community engagement and potential legal liabilities. For the Group, such disruptions may also harm the public perception of its operations, influencing brand reputation and, ultimately, customer loyalty, which could have financial consequences in the form of decreased demand or increased operational costs.

However, these same challenges present opportunities for the Group to mitigate risks and create value through proactive engagement with affected communities. By addressing community concerns and implementing measures to reduce noise, enhance aesthetics and preserve landscapes, the Group can build stronger relationships with residents and stakeholders. This not only reduces the risk of reputational damage but can also enhance Group's position as a responsible, sustainable operator. The trust Group builds through these actions can be translated into financial value, including long-term operational efficiency, stronger community support and potential incentives from local governments. Moreover, investing in sustainability initiatives can lead to cost savings in energy efficiency and waste management, further improving MOTOR OIL Group's financial performance while aligning with evolving regulatory and market demands.

The Group's ongoing commitment to providing affordable energy and ensuring energy security for communities with donations or own operations plays a vital role in promoting social stability, supporting economic growth and enhancing societal well-being - ultimately translating into tangible financial benefits.

Through both its operations and donations, the MOTOR OIL Group contributes to the energy supply that powers vital sectors such as healthcare, education and transportation - critical pillars of local economies. For instance, by providing electricity to hospitals, the Group enhances healthcare delivery and potentially reduce long-term healthcare costs. Supporting educational institutions with technology facilitates a more skilled workforce, contributing to economic growth. In the transportation sector, energy fuels improved efficiency and safety, directly benefiting industries reliant on logistics and infrastructure. These practices foster long-term economic growth and contribute to the creation of jobs, enhancing the Group's financial stability and strengthening its position in the marketplace.

##### 9.2 Impact, risk and opportunity management

###### 9.2.1 Policies related to Affected Communities [S3-1]

####### Community Contribution Policy

MOTOR OIL Group is committed to supporting the well-being and development of the communities in which it operates. To this end, the Group has established a Community Contribution Policy, ensuring that donations, sponsorships and community programs align with its corporate values and the United Nations Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs). The policy serves as a framework for assessing and implementing voluntary contributions that have a meaningful and lasting impact on society.

The policy ensures that all community contributions align with the Group's strategic priorities, sustainability goals and ethical principles. Contributions may include financial support, in-kind donations, or services to promote specific societal goals, always in accordance with the Group's Code of Ethics and Corporate Responsibility and Sustainable Development Strategy. CEO and Chairman of the BoD is responsible for the implementation of the policy.

In 2024, MOTOR OIL Group contributed nearly 6.3 million euros in donations and sponsorships, benefiting local communities. The Group's contributions are guided by its four Community Contribution Pillars, which focus on areas of significant social importance. These pillars are (1) Arts, Culture, Sports, (2) Environment, (3) Education and Innovation and (4) Local communities and Society and ensure that each initiative supported is strategically selected based on its potential to create positive change and address the needs of society effectively.

To ensure fairness and transparency in the allocation of resources, all requests for contributions must undergo a formal evaluation process. Interested organizations or individuals are required to complete a specific application form, which is then reviewed based on the criteria outlined in the Community Contribution Policy.

The policy should be read in conjunction with the Code of Ethics and Corporate Responsibility that specifically addresses human rights and working practices as well as the sustainability policy that includes MOTOR OIL Group's commitment to ongoing, transparent and meaningful dialogue with stakeholders (including local communities), ensuring that their concerns, expectations and insights are integrated into the company's sustainability strategy and decision-making processes.

Furthermore, the Whistleblowing mechanism of the Group may provide a remedy through a structured process if any human rights wrongdoing is reported.

Group's policies are rooted in internationally recognized standards, ensuring that its business practices align with globally accepted ethical and sustainability principles. The Group upholds the United Nations Universal Declaration of Human Rights, which serves as the foundation for its commitment to human dignity, equality and fair treatment across its workforce and operations. Additionally, as a signatory to the UN Global Compact, the Group integrates its ten principles into corporate governance, covering key areas such as human rights, labor rights, environmental responsibility and anti-corruption measures.


In addition, no cases have been reported on non-respect of the UN Guiding Principles on Business and Human Rights, ILO Declaration on Fundamental Principles and Rights at Work or OECD Guidelines for Multinational Enterprises that involve affected communities in Group's own operations. Finally, there are no indigenous people as defined by the ESRS in any part of MOTOR OIL Group's operations.

<!-- Report Page 246 -->
###### 9.2.2 Processes for Engaging with Affected Communities about Impacts [S3-2]

####### Affected communities' engagement

MOTOR OIL Group maintains open and accessible communication channels with affected communities, even though no material positive or negative impacts have been identified. This proactive approach reflects the Group's commitment to transparency, trust-building and ongoing dialogue with local stakeholders. By keeping these channels active, the Group ensures it remains responsive to community concerns and anticipates potential issues. In that sense MOTOR OIL Group have established multiple processes to engage with them.

Engagement with affected communities may occur directly through the general inquires section on MOTOR OIL's website (moh.gr/general-inquires) where anyone (either a legitimate representative or a credible proxy) has access and may initiate a communication process.

Within the context of Double Materiality Assessment (as described in ESRS-2 IRO-1), the Group has appointed a Third Party to collect insights, analyze stakeholder feedback and ensure a comprehensive and objective representation of stakeholder engagement in the double materiality scoring process.

As part of the community contribution policy, engagement may also occur through the Community Contribution Form where anyone can make a request for support and state the expected results from the contribution. In that sense an active engagement may start with the requestee, as part of the completion process, where specific insights can be gained and in certain circumstances understand how the Group can contribute to mitigate impacts. After evaluation by the Community Contributions Committee, the proposed contributions are submitted to the Chairman and CEO for final approval, ensuring alignment with the Group's values and strategic priorities.

The functions responsible for ensuring engagement with affected communities may fall under the Communication and Public Relations Division, Sustainability Division, Commercial Subsidiaries General Division and Refinery General Division.

Furthermore, the Group has developed a Stakeholder Engagement Plan (SEP) designed to enhance transparency, foster two-way communication and align MOTOR OIL Group with social, environmental and regulatory expectations. The plan involves analyzing the relationship between each stakeholder group and the company, identifying key interaction points, understanding the impacts and interdependencies and recognizing potential conflicts.

Particularly the refinery, has established a procedure for engaging with stakeholders from affected communities. This procedure outlines how MOTOR OIL Group handles communication, promotes information and facilitates feedback and suggestions. The stakeholders are divided into categories, including local government, state bodies, residents, customers, certification bodies, ecological associations, workers' institutions and suppliers.

The relevant department Head evaluates the communication with stakeholders. If the issue is important or requires further investigation, a summary form is filled, and a response letter is prepared in consultation with top management. For less significant matters, the relevant departments provide a response.

For corrective actions, regarding environmental concerns, MOTOR OIL Group outlines the following steps:

1\. If the communication relates to a recorded impact, the possibility of non-compliance with environmental management requirements is assessed.

2\. If the impact is unrecorded, the issue is reviewed in the Environmental Impact Register.

3\. Preventive and corrective actions are then activated.

4\. Any identified deviation is integrated into an Environmental Management Program for improvement.

###### 9.2.3 Processes to Remediate Negative Impacts and Channels for Affected Communities to Raise Concerns [S3-3]

During the double materiality assessment process, no negative material impacts have been emerged. The Group supports the availability of channels for stakeholders to raise concerns through several processes. The general approach for providing any kind of remedy is described in the several processes that the Group utilizes for raising concerns or complaints.

For example, in the retail sector a Request and Complaint Management Code has been in place where it describes the:. Methods for submitting customer requests or complaints.. Categorization of requests and complaints based on their subject.

· A report recording system with proof of entry (unique reference number).. The process for handling and investigating customer requests and complaints (responsible department, response deadlines, review stages, etc.).. The option for customers to appeal to recognized alternative dispute resolution bodies.

In the Refinery there is a specific process for Handling Complaints that includes the following:. Complaint Submission: Complaints are activated when the customer notifies the refinery and they must provide documentation (e.g., certificates from customs or independent inspectors).. Investigation: The complaint is investigated by the relevant departments and a standardized form is filled out to track the process.. Customer Communication: The customer is updated based on the findings and if technical issues are involved, corrective actions are proposed.. Negotiation: The relevant General Directorate negotiates with the customer for a resolution, which may include compensation or agreement withdrawal if the company is not at fault.. Record Keeping: Complaints are documented for up to five years.

Through the whistleblowing mechanism the Group encourages the submission of confidential or anonymous reports through the existing reporting channels about illegal activities that may take place in the organization. The management and investigation of these reports is conducted objectively, independently and with respect to all implicated parties. The Group assures that those who report through whistleblowing policy channels shall be protected against retaliation, which is expressly stated to be prohibited against the affected persons and that the personal data of all parties involved are protected.


<!-- Report Page 248 -->
##### 9.2.4 Taking Action on Material Impacts on Affected Communities, and Approaches to Managing Material Risks and Pursuing Material Opportunities related to Affected Communities, and Effectiveness of those Actions [S3-4]

MOTOR OIL Group hence strives to build constructive relationships with the local communities in which it operates, by fostering a relationship based on trust and mutual respect. More specifically, local communities are supported by the Group through programs that enhance economic, social and cultural life and overall help society thrive. The Group makes direct or indirect financial contributions to many businesses and families, while supporting local initiatives and encouraging entrepreneurship, in a general effort to improve the well-being of the communities in which it operates.

The Group's Sustainability Strategy on social welfare addresses both individual needs and societal goals in alignment with the 17 Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs), thereby affirming its commitment to addressing key societal challenges. This commitment is reflected in various initiatives and projects that the Group undertakes, ranging from educational programs, healthcare support, environmental conservation efforts, to cultural and sports sponsorships. The MOTOR OIL Group, through "Frontizo" program, a high-responsibility initiative with actions and projects that impact its world, aims for a better future.

In the realm of education, MOTOR OIL Group, fosters future growth through quality education and STEM training programs. In addition, healthcare is another critical area where MOTOR OIL Group makes significant contributions. The Group supports local public hospitals with donations and organizes health awareness campaigns to promote well-being among community members.

The Group undertakes various environmental conservation projects, such as reforestation, waste management and water preservation of coastal and ocean initiatives. By promoting sustainable practices and raising awareness about environmental issues, the Group aims to protect natural resources and ensure a sustainable future for generations to come.

Cultural and sports sponsorships are integral to the Group's community engagement. MOTOR OIL Group supports local cultural events, festivals and sports activities, which not only enrich the cultural fabric of the community but also promote social cohesion and a sense of belonging among residents. These sponsorships provide opportunities for local talent to be supported and foster a vibrant community spirit.

Moreover, MOTOR OIL Group's efforts to encourage entrepreneurship and digital skills are evident in its support for local startups and small businesses. The Group provides financial assistance, mentorship and networking opportunities to budding entrepreneurs, helping them turn their innovative ideas into successful ventures. This not only stimulates economic growth but also creates job opportunities and drives innovation within the community.

###### Refinery activities

MOTOR OIL HELLAS S.A. has invested in local communities, supporting organizations through donations, service projects and volunteer efforts for affected communities to maximize positive contributions. Through these investments, the Group enhances community well-being, promotes long-term sustainability and upholds human rights in its operational areas. It should be noted that in 2023, Motor Oil donated the amount of 50 million Euros to the Vardinoyannis Foundation. The Foundation is responsible for the financial management of this amount. Within this context, key initiatives include the donation of modern medical equipment to Corinth Hospital, thereby enhancing local healthcare infrastructure and the provision of a 1,600 m3 desalination unit to Agioi Theodoroi and a 200 m3 unit to Loutraki-Pera Chora to contribute to addressing the issue of water scarcity for local residents. The company has also made fuel donations to schools, fire protection associations and social organizations in municipalities such as Loutraki-Perachora-Agioi Theodoroi and Corinth, supporting critical services for education, emergency response and vulnerable groups.

In addition, MOTOR OIL HELLAS has partnered with the NGO W.I.N. HELLAS to contribute to the protection of women from gender-based violence and promote equal rights within affected communities. The company has fully reconstructed the sole residence on the remote island of Kinaros, ensuring dignified living conditions for the only resident, who faces extreme isolation and limited access to resources. Further initiatives include the donation of more than 24 automated external defibrillators (AEDs) to schools, sports clubs and public spaces to enhance emergency response capabilities and community safety.

The company has also supported bone marrow donor registration campaigns in collaboration with ORAMA ELPIDAS, encouraged community involvement in reforestation efforts in Agioi Theodoroi and constructed fire lookout stations in Sofiko and Chiliomodi in partnership with local fire protection groups to bolster community safety and wildfire prevention. MOTOR OIL HELLAS has made contributions to the national reforestation plan in 2024 (as well as in 2022 & 2023), including investments in forestry seed production facilities to support long-term ecological restoration efforts and the conservation of rare Mediterranean species.

Additionally, the company has advanced social cohesion and inclusivity through the "Painting School Buildings" program in collaboration with UrbanAct, transforming 10 schools in affected areas and the "Ready for Work @ Engineering" program, which provided professional development and mentorship to young women engineers across Greece. MOTOR OIL HELLAS has also strengthened community identity through cultural sponsorships, including support for the Athens Jazz Festival, Megaron Garden Summer Events and the Biennale of Contemporary Ceramics. In the realm of sports, the company has sponsored young athletes, including Olympians, to promote social mobility, youth engagement and equal access to professional development in sports.

Through these diverse initiatives, MOTOR OIL HELLAS continues to engage with local communities in a structured, collaborative manner, assessing needs and co-designing solutions with key stakeholders.

####### Circular Economy & Alternative Fuels Sector

THALIS ENVIRONMENTAL SERVICES SINGLE MEMBER S.A. has demonstrated a strong commitment to cultural enrichment, education and social welfare through a series of impactful initiatives that support local communities and vulnerable groups. L.P.C. S.A. has concentrated its efforts on community development in Aspropyrgos by supporting cultural and sports sponsorships, as well as social programs. Through its sponsorship of the Thriasian Cultural Events, the company has reinforced its connection with the local community. Additionally, L.P.C. has contributed to social welfare by donating essential goods to the Social Grocery Store of Aspropyrgos. Recognizing the importance of youth development, LPC has also invested in sports infrastructure by renovating the auxiliary field at Aspropyrgos Stadium, benefiting over 200 young athletes from local sports academies.

###### Retail activities

The company has implemented seasonal initiatives such as "Christmas for All", "Easter for All" and "Smiles for All", through which employees and volunteers have distributed essential supplies, festive gifts and school materials to families in need. These initiatives have provided meaningful assistance to marginalized populations while reinforcing a culture of corporate social responsibility.

In alignment with its commitment to environmental consciousness, NRG has funded climate awareness initiatives, including a swimming challenge undertaken by Spyros Chrysikopoulos, aimed at drawing attention to climate change and pollution.

###### Renewable Energy Sources activities

MORE has prioritized renewable energy education to foster awareness and innovation among young students. The company has launched an interactive STEM-based educational initiative on renewable energy sources, which has been implemented in 40 primary and secondary schools across Evia, Kozani and Monemvasia. Engaging approximately 2,400 students, the program has provided hands-on learning experiences designed to enhance understanding of sustainability and clean energy solutions. Through activities that encourage the exploration of renewable energy applications, students have gained valuable insights into how clean energy technologies contribute to environmental preservation and economic sustainability. By integrating STEM-based learning, MORE enhanced students' understanding of renewable energy, inspiring the next generation to embrace sustainable practices and contribute to the global energy transition.

##### Mitigation actions

The Group has implemented mitigation measures to address the environmental and social impacts of its industrial operations. Noise mitigation measures include the installation of sound barriers and noise-dampening equipment, as well as scheduled maintenance programs to minimize disturbances. Additionally, operational protocols limit high-noise activities to designated timeframes, ensuring compliance with noise control regulations.

To reduce the visual impact of industrial infrastructure, the Group integrates environmental and aesthetic design principles into its projects. This includes green buffers, landscaping initiatives and architectural modifications that blend industrial sites into the surrounding environment. Measures such as vegetation barriers and color harmonization are incorporated to minimize the visual footprint of facilities.

A key aspect of the Group's mitigation actions is sustainable land management, ensuring that industrial developments minimize ecosystem disruption. Environmental Impact Assessments (EIAs) are conducted prior to new projects, incorporating biodiversity preservation measures and cultural heritage protection. Engaging with stakeholders is fundamental to the Group's approach.

Structured consultations, community dialogue sessions and engagement mechanisms (as described above) ensure that local concerns are addressed proactively. Additionally, the Group invests in corporate responsibility initiatives, including infrastructure improvements that enhance community resilience and well-being.

Furthermore, the Group is collaborating with a third-party organization to develop a comprehensive Social Return on Investment (SROI) methodology. This initiative aims to enhance the accuracy and effectiveness of assessing the impact of its programs. By implementing this methodology, the Group will be able to measure and understand the social, environmental and economic value created through its initiatives, ensuring a more transparent and informed approach to its community engagement efforts.

Lastly, no severe human rights issues or incidents related to affected communities have been reported through the Whistleblowing communication channels.

###### Refinery activities

At the refinery level, mitigation efforts focus on site-specific impacts, particularly related to noise, visual disturbances and community concerns. MOTOR OIL HELLAS S.A. has established a formal complaints management procedure to handle environmental grievances from residents, ensuring a structured and transparent resolution process. Complaints related to noise and other disturbances can be received through telephone through the call center of from Refinery's Central Portal and recorded by designated gatekeepers and escalated to an environmental engineer, who assesses the issue and, when necessary, conducts on-site inspections to evaluate the situation firsthand. This hands-on approach enables MOTOR OIL HELLAS S.A. to identify patterns, assess risks and implement appropriate corrective actions. The Head of the Environmental Section is then informed and provides recommendations to senior management, ensuring that necessary measures are taken to mitigate the impact on the community.

Once an issue has been assessed, senior management oversees the implementation of corrective actions, allocating the necessary resources to ensure effective resolution. Additionally, the continuous monitoring and refinement of mitigation measures ensure compliance with regulatory requirements and sustainability commitments, strengthening the company's long-term environmental and social performance.

These efforts help mitigate the impact of industrial pollution on marine biodiversity and coastal livelihoods, ensuring that affected communities maintain access to clean water and healthy fisheries.

PRASINO LADI has actively promoted alternative fuels as a means of reducing the environmental footprint of the oil and gas sector. The company has prioritized the collection and recycling of used cooking oil, converting it into biodiesel as part of its broader strategy to advance cleaner fuel alternatives. By doing so, PRASINO LADI contributes to reducing air pollution and carbon emissions that negatively impact communities living near refineries and transportation corridors.

##### Retail activities

Through Project PARALIES, the company has taken responsibility for a section of Schinias Beach, working to mitigate pollution and marine debris accumulation. By actively collecting and recycling waste into upcycled products, AVIN has contributed to reducing environmental degradation, which can be exacerbated by industrial activities and oil-related waste.

The "YMoto Energy for the Care of Hymettus" initiative has involved the planting of 500 trees, with future commitments to mountain cleanups and the watering of 5,000 trees in collaboration with Let's Do It Greece. This program equips young individuals with the skills and awareness necessary to support the transition toward renewable energy, ultimately reducing reliance on traditional fossil fuels.

####### 9.2.5 Targets related to Managing Material Negative Impacts, Advancing Positive Impacts, and Managing Material Risks and Opportunities [S3-5]

MOTOR OIL Group is committed to establishing clear, measurable targets aimed at improving the wellbeing and resilience of communities affected by its operations. While the Group has not set specific targets directly related to affected communities the effectiveness of these actions is monitored and reviewed annually through the Community Contribution Policy, which defines a quantitative indicator \- specifying the percentage of EBITDA each company must allocate for community initiatives.

Although, the commitment of the Group to improving the well-being of the communities is evident, Group has not yet set specific targets due to being in the process of refining the Community Contribution framework. The Community Contribution Committee reports to the Sustainability Committee on the amount of the contribution made by the Group in the previous year together with the number of requests.

## 10 CONSUMERS AND END USERS [S4]

### 10.1 Strategy

#### 10.1.1 Material Impacts, Risks and Opportunities and their interaction with Strategy and Business Model [S4.SBM-3]

MOTOR OIL Group operates across a diverse range of business activities, serving both businesses and end consumers through its extensive value chain (please refer to 1.4.2 Group Structure paragraph "Value Chain").

The Group's operations include refining, storage, distribution and marketing of petrochemical products \- such as fuels and lubricants - while also offering electricity and natural gas sales, electromobility solutions, circular economy & alternative fuels and renewable energy. Within its Retail segment, the Group serves a broad spectrum of consumers, offering products - such as fuels, lubricants and LPG - as well as energy-related services such as electricity and gas supply. These activities position the Group as a key player in meeting the energy needs of individuals, businesses and industries, ensuring that its products and services are accessible, safe and aligned with evolving consumer expectations and respective regulations.

MOTOR OIL Group recognizes that consumers and end-users are key groups of affected stakeholders (further analysis regarding the stakeholders can be found in sub-Chapter: Interests and views of stakeholders [SBM-2]) and their interests, views and rights, including the respect for human rights (please refer to the Code of Ethics and Corporate Responsibility on the respect for human rights) are integral to shaping the company's strategy and business model. MOTOR OIL Group engages with them through structured dialogues, loyalty programs and feedback mechanisms (Questionnaires).

In the retail segment, the following types of consumers and end-users fall within the scope of the Group's quality process of products and services:

1\. Fuel Station Owners: One key group is fuel station owners, who rely on the consistent quality of fuels and services to operate their stations and serve their customers effectively

2\. Industrial Clients: Companies and organizations that procure fuels and other products for their industrial production, being affected by the quality processes applied at all stages of production and delivery.

3\. End Customers/Consumers: Individuals or households who purchase fuels, non-fuel offerings, which include coffee products, mini-markets, car accessories, lubricants and services from fuel stations.

4\. Customers Purchasing Heating Oil: Consumers who buy heating oil, relying on the quality and reliability of the products for their heating needs.

5\. Electricity Consumers: Households and businesses supplied with electricity, depending on the reliability and efficiency of the energy services provided.

Recognizing the importance of consumer protection, product safety and regulatory compliance, MOTOR OIL Group adheres to European legislation on product labeling (CLP Regulation (EC) No 1272/2008). Retail companies ensure that all necessary classifications and product information are clearly communicated to consumers. These efforts support informed decision-making and strengthen public confidence in the Group's products.

To maintain high-quality standards and prevent the distribution of substandard products, the refinery and retail companies implement appropriate quality control measures. These controls are essential in ensuring safer product use, reducing potential health risks and reinforcing consumer trust. Beyond direct costumers, quality assurance processes reinforce transparency and credibility, upholding high standards that enhance trust and reliability for business partners and suppliers across the Group's value chain. By embedding these controls into its operations, the Group reinforces its reputation as a responsible and consumer-focused organization, committed to delivering safe, high-quality and sustainable energy solutions.

Based on the outcomes of the Double Materiality Assessment (DMA) (Chapter from ESRS 2: Impact, risk and opportunity management), the analysis indicates that there are no material impacts (or risks) on consumers or end-users.

##### Opportunities

MOTOR OIL Group recognizes that maintaining high product quality and safety standards presents a material opportunity to enhance consumer trust and strengthen its market position. This opportunity relates to all consumers and end users of the Group. By implementing appropriate quality control measures (e.g. provision of material safety data sheets, compliance with all mandatory regulations, appropriate packaging and labeling of products that provides advice on their safe storage, use and disposal) the Group can proactively prevent financial losses associated with product recalls, legal liabilities and potential reputational damage. These efforts contribute to a more resilient business model, reducing operational risks while maintaining trusted relationships with consumers, business partners and regulatory authorities. Given the immediate impact of quality assurance measures, this opportunity is classified within a short-term time frame, allowing for quick adaptation and measurable improvements in operational performance.

Beyond risk mitigation, a quality assurance framework (e.g. ISO 9001:2015, ISO 17025:2017) serves as a competitive advantage, driving consumer preference and brand loyalty. Since there is a clear dependency on consumers, enhanced product safety and reliability can lead to higher customer satisfaction, increased sales growth and expanded market share by consistently delivering high-quality, safe and compliant products. In the short term, these initiatives enable the Group to respond swiftly to market demands and regulatory requirements, ensuring immediate value creation while laying the groundwork for sustained consumer trust.

### 10.2 Personal safety of consumers and/or end-users | Policies, actions and targets

#### 10.2.1 Policies and Actions related to Consumers and End-Users [S4-1] [S4-4]

MOTOR OIL Group integrates consumer safety, product quality and regulatory compliance across all aspects of its operations, ensuring high safety standards throughout its value chain. These principles are embedded in its Policies & Commitments with regards to Quality, which establishes a structured framework applied to production, distribution and customer interactions. Examples of these policies are the Quality Policy of MOTOR OIL and the Product Stewardship Policy of Coral. These are available upon request by the customer/end user. It should be noted though, that the ISO related to quality are available through the websites of Group's companies.

A key element in maintaining these high standards is the ISO 9001:2015-certified Quality Management System (QMS). Senior management oversees its implementation, setting measurable objectives and conducting regular performance reviews to drive continuous improvement. The report will be centrally managed, with support from the Customer Service Center (CSC) Manager.

By aligning with international safety standards (e.g. 14001:2015, 45001:2018, 50001:2018, 9001:2015) and best industry practices, the Group reinforces its commitment to consumer well-being and product integrity. Beyond product quality, MOTOR OIL Group prioritizes transparent customer feedback management through its various Customer Complaints Policies and processes (e.g. Customer Complaints Policy & Procedures, Customer complaints processes in the Refinery, code for the management of requests and complaints for electricity customers). These policies ensure a structured, proactive approach to addressing customer concerns, driving service enhancements and implementing corrective actions based on consumer insights. The complaint policy is organized into two main cycles: the complaint logging and resolution cycle and the complaint analysis and learning cycle. In the complaint logging and resolution cycle, when a customer expresses dissatisfaction with the product or service received (from email, call center or social media) the complaint is captured, preferably through an automated system. The issue is then resolved within 5 calendar days and the resolution is communicated back to the customer.

In the complaint analysis and learning cycle, complaints are reported on, a monthly basis. An in-depth analysis is conducted to determine the root causes of the complaints received. Based on this analysis, corrective actions are implemented to prevent the issue from recurring and the insights gained are captured. These learnings are then shared within the organization to drive continuous improvement.

Systematic monitoring and analysis of feedback allow the Group to continuously refine its services and maintain high levels of customer satisfaction. One of the commitments of the Quality Policy of MOTOR OIL, is to understand the needs and expectations of its customers and of any interested party and respond promptly to the requirements of its customers as well as to meet the commitments and obligations of the Group, as identified by the relevant contractual agreements. Furthermore, MOTOR OIL, through constant communication with its customers and end users (Chapter (ESRS 2): Interests and views of stakeholders [SBM-2]) and the relevant to customers concerns, takes into consideration their views in drafting the relevant policies. With regards to the Customers Complaints policies and procedures, these available upon request or provided directly through the websites of the relevant companies.

Health & Safety Regulations are applied. Retail companies, through ISO 45001:2018 certifications, systematically assess health and safety (H&S) risks, impacts and controls, continuously updating its H&S Risk and Controls Assessment. Staff and contractors are encouraged to report actual and potential incidents through dedicated web-based platform, with all safety committee reports, peer reviews, internal audits and customer service findings recorded for ongoing monitoring and reassessment.

To reinforce responsible business conduct, MOTOR OIL Group upholds a Regulatory Framework Violation Reporting Policy ("Whistleblowing Policy"), allowing employees, customers and business partners to report regulatory violations confidentially and without fear of retaliation. This aligns with the Group's Code of Ethics and Corporate Responsibility, ensuring adherence to ethical business practices and respect for internationally recognized human rights (for detailed analysis of these policies, refer to chapter Working conditions | Policies and actions [S1-1] [S1-4]).

Ensuring consumer trust through quality control, the Group utilizes specialized laboratories and mobile quality control units for product inspections. These labs support refinery, port and tanker loading operations by conducting extensive tests on incoming crude oil, chemicals and additives, as well as final products before distribution. Monthly lab tests ensure both product excellence and safe distribution, minimizing potential risks to human health and the environment. This process is taking place in the whole value chain (upstream, own operations and downstream) and across all geographies where the group operates. Stakeholders that are affected include consumer and end users, as well as Business partners. The resources that are used by the Group include mainly personnel from quality departments.

<!-- Report Page 258 -->
# Retail activities

AVIN OIL aims to foster trust and assurance for the customer in each of its service stations throughout Greece. The company has created a Quality & Quantitative Control Department as well as the program «AVIN Quality Assurance» to ensure the quality of AVIN OIL fuel. In summary, the program includes:

1\. Fuel Pump Quality Control: Samples are taken from all pump nozzles of each service station and quality control is performed on each product. The characteristics that are checked are: specific gravity, appearance, color and clarity, control for moisture or particles or foreign impurities / substances, state tracers and control of gasoline with a portable «Octanemeter» Mid-IR, which shows the number of Octane (RON) as well as for the detection of other chemicals in gasoline.

2\. Quantitative Control of Fuel Pumps: The quantity received by the consumer is important to be correct in each supply. AVIN OIL technicians test the pumps with accredited 20-liter measuring containers and confirm that the pumps deliver the right amount, at each refueling. In parallel with the control of pumps, the good operation of the liter meters of the gas stations is checked daily, so that the staff can correctly measure the pumps of the service station.

3\. Proper Sealing of Fuel Pumps: Fuel pumps are adjusted and sealed appropriately. AVIN OIL technicians confirm that the sealing of the pumps is appropriate in accordance with the national legislative framework and that no pump has been tampered with.

4\. Technical Inspection of Wells, Tanks and Quality Inspection of Products in Fuel Tanks: The condition of gas station equipment (tanks, wells) is inspected monthly, including product quality control (e.g. detection of water, particles and foreign substances). This ensures that the correct storage and handling practices at AVIN OIL stations are always followed. and certified by BUREAU VERITAS, an independent, internationally, recognized, quality certification body.

Coral has implemented quality management processes across all its activities in compliance with the ISO 9001:2015 standard. It also ensures quality control for the products and services offered, as well as operational safety at its network of owned fuel stations, particularly at highway stations (SEAs), through a certified Quality Management System by TUV Austria. Additionally, the company maintains a certified Quality Management System by Bureau Veritas Certification (BV Cert.) for the entire supply chain, including receiving, storage, mixing, loading of transport vehicles and the delivery of petroleum, lubricants and chemicals to customers. Furthermore, it monitors and evaluates the effectiveness of its actions through a comprehensive system for the quantitative and qualitative control of fuels throughout transportation and storage. Customer satisfaction is tracked through regular surveys and feedback, which help identify areas for improvement. The company also monitors sales and usage data to assess product and service success, while evaluating the quality of service provided at fuel stations and other points of sale. Adhering to strict quality, health, safety and environmental standards, Coral maintains relevant ISO certifications (e.g.14001:2015, 45001:2018, 50001:2018, 9001:2015). Furthermore, it continuously invests in upgrading operations and equipment to enhance service delivery and minimize environmental impact.

LPC abides with the relevant European legislation (CLP Regulation (EC) No 1272/2008) placing the necessary information and classifications on all product labels as well as publishing online all Market Safety Data Sheets of products. Furthermore, with the "Lab Project" LPC successfully monitors the sampling of packaged products, printing barcode labels for samples, recording chemistry analysis results and implementing corrective actions. A mobile application is utilized to support field usage decisions, with the added functionality of photo capture for documentation purposes.

No severe human rights issues or incidents connected to consumers and end-users have been identified within the reporting period through the whistleblowing communication channels. Through the Code of Ethics and Corporate Responsibility which applies in both own workforce and third parties, is stated that Group respects and supports internationally recognised human rights. The Group, within its sphere of influence, supports and respects the protection of human rights and ensures that any type of violation or infringement thereof is avoided.

## 10.2.2 Processes for Engaging with Consumers and End-Users about Impacts [S4-2]

Engagement with consumers and end users may occur directly through the general inquires section of MOTOR OIL Group's website, where anyone (either a legitimate representative or a credible proxy) has access and may initiate a communication process. General inquiries sections are also available on the websites of its retail companies. Through this channel, the communication can be directed towards several internal functions. Accordingly, the Relevant department, is responsible for overseeing this process.

Furthermore, the Group has developed a Stakeholder Engagement Plan (SEP) designed to enhance transparency, foster two-way communication and align MOTOR OIL with social, environmental and regulatory expectations. The plan involves analyzing the relationship between each stakeholder group and the Group, identifying key interaction points, understanding the impacts and interdependencies and recognizing potential conflicts. The sustainability Committee is responsible for overseeing the process.

In MOTOR OIL Group, customer's needs are priority, providing them with a notable customer service experience. MOTOR OIL's refinery has established an assessment process of the customer service provided, in order, to promptly identify any wrong acts and consequently, improve its performance and ensure clients' satisfaction.

Under this framework, the Group:

· Conducts quantitative & qualitative customer satisfaction surveys. Identifies and evaluates its strengths and weaknesses

· Manages the identified flaws. Improves its tactics and performance

This whole process aims to the minimization of complaints relevant to MOTOR OIL Group's products. The General Directorate of Supply & Trading is responsible for overseeing the process. An indicative example with regards to the customer service approach comes from Group's subsidiary NRG:

NRG Customer Experience (CXP) consists of Customer Support, Digital Channels (e.g. chat, email handling) and Complaints Management (e.g. written complaints).

NRG CXP aims at:

1\. Mapping customer journeys, analyzing information concerning customer insights and expectations to identify opportunities to proactively amend and enhance current processes.

2\. Ensuring that each touchpoint across the customer journey is engaging, efficient, effective and provides consistent cxp.

3\. Ensigning actions, customer moments, targeting to increase customer satisfaction rates, customer loyalty and retention and to reduce churn. Systematically monitors process enhancements.

4\. Cascading the agreed customer experience strategy across all company touchpoints.

5\. Monitoring KPIs progress and productively utilizes all available resources.

6\. Analyzing quantitative and qualitative results, drive conclusions, design roadmap of actions to meet customers' expectations.

7\. Defining and driving adoption of best practices around customer engagement in energy industry.

Some extra landmarks:. Loyalty scheme for customers via NRG all smart since 2019 and other loyalty schemes (health card, gift card).. Quality Assurance Specialist since 2020 in order, to evaluate Agents' calls.

· Member of Hellenic Institute of Customer Service since 2019.

· IVR customer survey since 2021 via 18101.. "MYnrg" application to easily access and manage data and information regarding meters, payments and invoices.. Various payment methods to facilitate customers' payments.

### 10.2.3 Processes to Remediate Negative Impacts and Channels for Consumers and End-Users to Raise Concerns [S4-3]

MOTOR OIL Group is committed to maintaining transparent, efficient and accessible communication channels for consumers and end-users, ensuring that they can voice concerns, submit complaints and receive timely resolutions. The Group has established a structured and systematic complaint management framework, designed to align with international best practices and regulatory requirements, reinforcing its customer-centric approach.

Consumers and end-users of MOTOR OIL Group's retail companies can submit complaints through multiple communication channels, including:

· Telephone support, offering direct and immediate assistance.. Email submissions, ensuring documented and trackable communication.

· Social media platforms (e.g., Facebook, CoralNet), enhancing accessibility and responsiveness.

· Voice of the Customer (VOC) platforms, providing structured feedback mechanisms.

All complaints are logged into the Salesforce system, ensuring centralized documentation and efficient tracking. They are then directed to the appropriate department for assessment and resolution. Quality and quantity-related complaints undergo additional review by specialized teams, including quality control officers, inspectors and operations managers, ensuring accurate evaluation and fair resolution.

To enhance transparency and accountability, a structured monthly reporting process is followed, which includes:

<!-- Report Page 262 -->
1\. Analysis of complaints received, allowing trend identification and continuous improvement.

2\. Monitoring unresolved complaints, ensuring that no issue remains unaddressed.

3\. Year-to-date statistical reports, providing insights into consumer concerns and operational enhancements.

Additionally, as part of its Consumer Complaints Policy, the Group implements measures to proactively engage with consumers throughout the resolution process. Customers receive timely updates regarding the status of their complaint, the investigation process and expected resolution timelines.

Furthermore, the Group has established procedures for handling complaints related to product quality, delivery and service performance. Special attention is given to customer complaints concerning the Chemistry Department, which adheres to ISO/IEC 17025:2017 standards to ensure the reliability and accuracy of laboratory analyses. This approach underscores the Group's commitment to continuous improvement, regulatory compliance and consumer protection across all its business segments.

MOTOR OIL Group also assesses whether consumers and end-users are aware of and trust these reporting structures. This is achieved through surveys, feedback mechanisms and direct stakeholder engagement sessions. The insights gathered help refine Group's approach, ensuring that individuals feel confident using these channels to communicate their concerns.

Furthermore, the Group has policies in place to protect individuals from any form of retaliation when utilizing these channels (please refer to the Chapter, "Processes to remediate negative impacts and channels for affected communities to raise concerns [$3-3]" for additional information regarding for additional information on the channels to raise concerns).

MOTOR OIL Group's non-retaliation approach guarantees that all reports are handled impartially and that those who raise concerns are safeguarded against any adverse actions. This commitment fosters an environment of trust, encouraging proactive communication and ethical business conduct. By maintaining multiple, well-defined complaint resolution channels and continuously refining its processes, MOTOR OIL Group reinforces its commitment to consumer protection, transparency and service excellence across all its business segments.

Below there is an example of a structured complaint resolution framework in the retail sector:

NRG applies a structured complaint resolution framework aligned with the regulatory requirements of the Electricity Supply Code (DEK B' 832/9.4.2013), which outlines:. Various complaint submission methods, including online forms, telephone support and written correspondence.. Complaint categorization and tracking, ensuring efficient issue resolution.. Defined response timelines and escalation procedures, ensuring prompt handling and regulatory compliance.. Alternative dispute resolution mechanisms, including referral to independent authorities such as the Regulatory Authority for Energy (RAE) and the Consumer Ombudsman.

#### 10.2.4 Targets related to Managing Material Negative Impacts, Advancing Positive Impacts, and Managing Material Risks and Opportunities [S4-5]

MOTOR OIL Group is committed to supporting the transition to sustainable mobility by significantly expanding its electromobility infrastructure, aiming to install over 4,000 charging points by 2030 (from ~1,550 in the H1 2024) aligning with Group's Strategy outlook. The methodologies used for setting the targets included scenarios and data sources such as EV adoption trends, charging demand (market growth), national targets under the National Energy and Climate Plan (NECP) and geographic factors, with assumptions about government incentives and regulation, advancements in EV technology and infrastructure availability. Various stakeholders, including industry experts, local authorities, consumer groups and retail networks were consulted to ensure the target is aligned with the demand. Progress is tracked through metrics like the number of charging points installed annually (1,629 charging points in 2024).

Consumer engagement was carried out through feedback surveys. This initiative is designed to meet the growing demand for electric vehicle (EV) charging solutions, ensuring greater accessibility and convenience for consumers and end-users. Through its subsidiaries, including NRG, AVIN OIL and CORAL, the Group is developing a reliable and efficient charging network, catering to both private consumers and commercial fleets. In future reporting periods, the Group intends to examine establishing more specific targets related to the topic.
