# 11 BUSINESS CONDUCT [G1]

## 11.1 Governance

### 11.1.1 The Role of the Administrative, Management and Supervisory Bodies [ESRS 2 GOV-1]

MOTOR OIL Group's governance structure is designed to uphold integrity, transparency and accountability in all aspects of business conduct. The Board of Directors plays a pivotal role in defining the Group's values and strategic direction while ensuring compliance with legal, regulatory and corporate governance standards. As part of this commitment, the Group adheres to the Hellenic Corporate Governance Code, implementing its principles through a "comply or explain" approach and providing explanations when deviations occur.

The Board operates under a well-defined Charter and oversees key areas such as shareholder rights, decision-making transparency, risk management and personnel policies. Additionally, the Sustainability Committee, functioning under the highest governance body, is responsible for shaping and monitoring the Group's Sustainability Policy and Strategy. With regular training on sustainability and corporate governance matters, the Committee ensures that the Board remains informed and equipped to address emerging risks and opportunities. Through these governance mechanisms, MOTOR OIL Group fosters responsible business conduct, reinforcing its commitment to ethical leadership and long-term sustainable growth.

The supervisory, management and administrative bodies involved with business conduct matters are the following:

· Supervisory bodies: Board of Directors (BoD) and Sustainability Committee. Management Bodies: Chief Executive Officer (CEO) and Deputy CEOs

· Administrative Bodies: Reports Management Committee (R.M.C), HR General Division - HR Dept, Head of Legal Dept, Compliance Unit, Internal Audit Unit

Their roles are described in the following sections: Chapter "Business conduct policies and corporate culture [G1-1]" and Chapter "Prevention and detection of corruption and bribery [G1-3]".

This chapter should be read in conjunction with ESRS 2 GOV-1 and Chapter "Prevention and detection of corruption and bribery [G1-3]". For additional information regarding the Committees and the expertise of the management and supervisory bodies on business conduct matters, please refer to Chapter "Corporate Governance Statement (L.4548/2018), The Charter of the Company's Board of Directors, Director's Suitability Policy and the Operating Regulations. Furthermore, reference should be made to the Remuneration & Nomination Committee where among its main responsibilities is to identify suitable candidates to become Directors, as well as to propose candidates to fill vacancies on the Board or its committees and to evaluate periodically the membership headcount and composition of the Board and its committees.

## 11.2 Strategy

### 11.2.1 Material Impacts, Risks and Opportunities

MOTOR OIL Group places anti-corruption and ethical business practices at the core of its operations, recognizing the immediate financial and reputational consequences of bribery and fraud. Legal penalties, loss of business contracts and diminished stakeholder confidence can directly impact profitability, making compliance a strategic necessity. Thus, beyond regulatory compliance, supplier governance plays a crucial role in maintaining financial and operational stability.

By ensuring structured oversight and transparency across its supply chain, MOTOR OIL Group reduces exposure to regulatory fines, legal liabilities and reputational damage. This approach not only ensures seamless business continuity but also fosters trust among stakeholders, reinforcing the company's resilience in an evolving market.

Furthermore, the Group's Whistleblowing Policy enhances corporate resilience, acting as a proactive measure to prevent fraud and reinforce ethical responsibility. Providing employees and stakeholders with a structured mechanism to report concerns minimizes the likelihood of costly legal actions while strengthening internal accountability. Over the mid-term, such initiatives contribute to a corporate culture of transparency, attracting investors who prioritize governance excellence and employees who seek a workplace built on integrity.

However, ethical business conduct is not just about mitigating risks - it is also a source of strategic advantage. MOTOR OIL Group's integrated compliance frameworks, which include stringent anticorruption measures, supplier governance and whistleblower protections, do more than ensure adherence to legal requirements. They create a foundation for long-term resilience, enhancing the company's reputation as a trusted industry leader while reinforcing its commitment to responsible business practices.

## 11.3 Impact, risk and opportunity management

### 11.3.1 Business Conduct Policies and Corporate Culture [G1-1]

Upholding the highest ethical standards is essential to maintaining trust with MOTOR OIL Group's stakeholders and ensuring sustainable business growth. To this end, the Group has established a comprehensive framework of policies and procedures that reinforce responsible business conduct and regulatory compliance. These include among others,. Code of Ethics and Corporate Responsibility. Anti-Bribery and Anti-Corruption Policy. Policy against violence and Harassment at work

· Whistleblowing Policy. Corporate Governance Code

The Code of Ethics and Corporate Responsibility aims to contribute to the further promotion of moral integrity, honesty, transparency, as well as proper professional behavior at all levels of the Group's hierarchy, so on the one hand, it orderly operation is preserved and on the other hand, the proper management of MOTOR OIL Group's business activities in the best interests of its employees, customers and shareholders is ensured. The Code is addressed to all employees and teams of the Group Companies as well as to any third parties or business partners who represent any Group or work on their behalf. It is applied in conjunction with legal and regulatory requirements as well as other approved policies and procedures of the Group. In absolute compliance with the Code of Ethics and Corporate Responsibility, the Group operates a corporate governance code which involves a set of relationships among a Group's management, its shareholders, employees and other stakeholders; and aims to create, sustain and develop strong and competitive companies.

Furthermore, the Group's parent company and its significant subsidiaries, according to Greek Law 4706/2020, as in force, have established their own Operating Regulations, which describe the organizational structure of the relevant Company as well as the scope of its divisions and include the policies and procedures, which are mandatory content under the current regulatory framework. Among others, the Operating Regulations include Compliance and Training Policies. According to these the Group organizes compliance training programs/awareness sessions which are monitored, planned and executed by the Human Resources General Division, while the Compliance Unit provides, if requested, support and guidance. Furthermore, the Compliance Unit carries out trainings/updates to the members of the Board of Directors and its Committees as well as to the persons discharging managerial responsibilities regarding their duties and responsibilities mainly regarding regulatory compliance and corporate governance issues.

Regarding compliance, the Group has established an independent Compliance Unit since 2022. During the reporting period, the Compliance Unit strengthened its operation and efficiency and guided the ongoing effort of the assurance of the Group compliance and the promotion of business conduct and corporate culture.

In compliance with EU Directive 2019/1937 and Greek Law 4990/2022, as in force, each Group company, that is obliged, appointed an Officer Responsible for the Receipt and Monitoring of Reports (ORRMR) in 2023 to independently manage investigations and report directly to the Board of Directors. The Group encourages employees and stakeholders to submit confidential or anonymous reports, ensuring protection from retaliation and data privacy in line with ethical governance.

More specifically the mechanism for identifying, reporting and investigating concerns about unlawful behavior is described in both the Whistleblowing policy and the code of ethics and corporate responsibility of the group which interact. In the event of indications of policy violations, employees must promptly inform the Investigation Team, following the guidelines outlined in the Whistleblowing Policy. All reports will be thoroughly reviewed, and corrective and remedial actions will be taken as needed. Reports can be made anonymously and individuals who report violations are protected from any potential retaliation.

The Group encourages the submission of confidential or anonymous reports through the existing reporting channels about illegal activities that may take place in the organization. The management and investigation of these reports is conducted objectively, independently and with respect to all implicated parties. The Group is committed that employees who have made a report will not suffer retaliation, harassment or marginalization, intimidation or threats and unfair treatment because of their report, (e.g. exclusion from training courses, non-approval of expenses, denial of leave, unfounded negative evaluation, etc.). Also, unjustified changes in the employment relationship because of the report are not allowed. In the case of a malicious report, the above protection does not apply. The same level of protection also applies to third persons connected to the Reporting Persons who could be retaliated against in a work context, such as colleagues or relatives of the Reporting Persons. Where the Reporting Persons is an external contractor, early termination, or cancellation of a contract for goods or services because of the report is not permitted.

The Group has established multiple reporting channels. Any employee, external partner or other person falling within the scope of the Whistleblowing Policy as well as the Policy against Violence and Harassment at Work suspecting that a wrongdoing (as mentioned at the above Policies) is occurring may contact the Officer Responsible for the Receipt and Monitoring of Reports (ORRMR) or the Compliance Officer (for the Group companies that have not designated an ORRMR) and in any case to communicate by name or anonymously through the following communication channels:

#### Whistleblowing Platform: whistleblowing.moh.gr. Written report - Letter marked "attention of the ORRMR or of the Compliance Officer (for Group companies that have not appointed an ORRMR)" or "Reference of Law 4808/2021 or Law 4990/2022" to the address: Irodou Attikou 12A, 151 24, Marousi, Greece. Personal meeting with the ORRMR or with the Compliance Officer (for Group companies that have not appointed an ORRMR) upon submission of a request in writing or orally or via email to the ORRMR or the Compliance Officer respectively.

<!-- Report Page 268 -->
Furthermore, if a whistleblowing report is received by an unauthorized person, they are obliged to forward

it to the ORRMR immediately, without any modification of its content or disclosure of information that may lead to the identification of the whistleblower, or any third party named in the report, in compliance with the provisions of Law 4990/2022. In 2024, no whistleblowing reports have been submitted.

In NRG, for customer concerns, specific grievance processes have been implemented, such as the "Requests & Complaints Management Code," which outlines submission methods, classification of complaints, response timelines and escalation options to out-of-court dispute resolution bodies. This comprehensive framework ensures a safe, transparent environment for raising concerns without fear of retaliation.

The HR Department, in collaboration with the Human Resources General Division, is responsible for ensuring that Group employees are informed and trained on its contents. This is achieved through various communication channels, including information materials, emails, newsletters and other suitable methods based on employee categories. The Policy is also prominently displayed on the Group's intranet and online reporting platform. Furthermore, awareness initiatives may be shared with relevant stakeholders - including investors, financial institutions, society and business partners - to reinforce the Group's commitment to integrity, honesty and transparency.

The Group's training policy on business conduct is designed to educate members of the Board of Directors, management and other executives. These training programs cover key areas such as competition law, anti-corruption measures and ethical business practices. A strong emphasis is placed on ensuring that all employees - particularly those handling high-risk transactions - can identify potential fraud, bribery, or other forms of corruption and understand the appropriate course of action in such situations. Training sessions on these topics are conducted annually, with the Human Resources General Division overseeing their development and implementation. Additionally, the Compliance Unit provides support when needed, ensuring that employees are well-prepared to uphold the highest standards of integrity and ethical conduct.

The Sustainability Committee undergoes regular training on various Sustainable Development topics to remain informed about the latest advancements. The Group's Board ensures that both the initial onboarding and ongoing training of its members align with the procedures recommended by the Remuneration & Nomination Committee. This continuous development enhances each Committee member's ability to assess and address the impacts, risks and opportunities within the ESG framework. The Board of Directors (BoD) stays well-informed on these matters and following discussions with the Sustainability Committee, approves necessary actions. Additionally, the Committee remains actively engaged in monitoring the outcomes of materiality assessments and performance evaluations in specific ESG areas.

The compliance training sessions for 2024 included, among others, important topics related to corporate ethics and responsibility. In Q1/2024, training focused on the Code of Conduct & Corporate Responsibility, addressing issues such as corruption and bribery. In Q2/2024, employees underwent training on Whistleblowing, which includes the procedures for reporting misconduct, as well as instances of violations of the Code of Conduct. Finally, in Q4/2024, there was a training session for the members of the Board of Directors and its Committees as well as to the persons discharging managerial responsibilities on Conflict-of-Interest issues, aimed at ensuring decisions are made based on the company's interests, not personal ones. These sessions reflected a strong commitment to promoting ethical practices and maintaining a healthy work environment. For 2024, all functions within the Group are considered to have similar level of risk concerning corruption and bribery.

### 11.3.2 Management of Relationships with Suppliers [G1-2]

MOTOR OIL Group embraces a collaborative approach that fosters mutually beneficial relationships with its partners and suppliers, built on a foundation of trust, integrity and transparency. Recognizing the critical role of its supply chain in driving sustainable development, the Group is committed to minimizing social and environmental risks while setting a positive example for the industry and broader community.

As part of its supplier evaluation process, MOTOR OIL Group assesses candidates not only based on conventional factors such as cost, quality and delivery but also on QHSSE (Quality, Health, Safety, Security and Environment) and ESG (Environmental, Social and Governance) criteria. With a diverse supplier base - including raw material providers, contractors, service providers, equipment manufacturers and infrastructure partners - the Group prioritizes working with suppliers who share its commitment to sustainability and ethical business practices.

Currently, the Group is developing a Suppliers & Business Partners Code of Conduct alongside a Responsible Supply Chain Policy to reinforce ethical and sustainable practices throughout its supply chain. The Code of Conduct, designed for third parties such as business partners and suppliers, defines key contractual obligations and requires compliance with its principles. It also encourages partners to ensure that their suppliers, contractors and subcontractors adhere to the same standards. Meanwhile, the Responsible Supply Chain Policy highlights the Group's commitment to working with partners who uphold the values of sustainability, integrity, transparency and human rights protection, further strengthening the Group's responsible business practices.

The upcoming policy highlights the Group's approach to creating a sustainable value chain that not only addresses social and environmental risks but also aims to set a standard for ethical practices within the industry. By communicating the Supplier and Business Partners Code of Conduct and promoting sustainability, the Group emphasizes its commitment to responsible practices across its supply chain. The emphasis on human rights, legal compliance and the avoidance of illegal materials highlights a strong stance on integrity. Moreover, the Group actively integrates Environmental, Social and Governance (ESG) criteria into its supplier selection and evaluation processes, ensuring that partners align with these standards. The training and educational efforts for both employees and suppliers reflect an ongoing commitment to upholding ethical values, while the systematic collection of data and use of whistleblowing mechanisms serve as tools for accountability. Ultimately, the Group's policy of terminating partnerships that violate legal, ethical, or ESG guidelines underscores a zero-tolerance approach to non-compliance, reinforcing the importance of adhering to both local and international standards in sustaining a responsible and ethical value chain.

MOTOR OIL Group has established a comprehensive supplier and contractor evaluation process, ensuring strict compliance with quality, safety and sustainability standards. This multi-stage assessment begins with an initial review of submitted documentation, followed by on-site evaluations when further scrutiny is required or recommended by evaluation participants. These measures guarantee that all suppliers meet the Group's operational and ESG (Environmental, Social and Governance) requirements.

Through the structured Purchasing Organization, retail companies have successfully maintained longterm partnerships with zero rejections while securing high-quality suppliers. Similarly, companies within the Circular Eco & Alt. Fuels evaluates suppliers based on their ability to deliver products and services that meet defined quality specifications. In some cases, on-site inspections are conducted at raw material suppliers' facilities to identify potential gaps in production, warehousing, or distribution processes.

Companies in the RES sector also emphasize supporting local communities by collaborating with local contractors for most of its projects, while simultaneously engaging with large international suppliers to ensure the implementation of ISO 14001, 50001 and 9001 certifications, wherever applicable. MOTOR OIL Group evaluates its RES Key Suppliers (Critical) through a three-stage process: prequalification, assessment during the tender process and post-performance evaluation.

Vendors providing critical materials or services must complete a prequalification questionnaire covering management standards such as ISO certifications, child labor policies and sanctions, though no formal assessment is conducted at this stage. Major vendors are post-evaluated based on their field performance, with a particular focus on QHSSE criteria, especially health and safety

Specific entities within the Group have additional supplier requirements,. receiving assurances from suppliers that contractors have fulfilled employee contribution payments and adhere to environmental regulations for projects within company facilities.. following a structured pre-cooperation assessment, including an HSSE (Health, Safety, Security and Environment) questionnaire and on-site verification by an external partner. Additionally, Retail mandates:

<!-- Report Page 270 -->
\- Compliance with ISO 14001 and other relevant ISO certifications.

\- Annual contractor meetings for HSSE updates.

\- Inclusion of contractor safety regulations in all tender documents.

By implementing these supplier assessment and compliance procedures, MOTOR OIL Group strives for operational excellence, environmental responsibility and sustainable partnerships across its supply chain.

### 11.3.3 Prevention and Detection of Corruption and Bribery [G1-3]

The Group is, committed to conducting business in accordance with the highest ethical standards and shows zero-tolerance in all forms of bribery and corruption. For that reason, the Group has set in force an Anti-Bribery and Anti-Corruption Policy and with great responsibility and business integrity, is committed to endeavor the utmost efforts for securing transparency and legitimacy.

Greece has ratified the UN Convention against Corruption with the Law 3666/2008, amending the Greek Penal Code. According to MOTOR OIL's Anti-bribery and Anti-Corruption Policy "The Policy abides by the applicable principles and provisions of the Greek Law and especially of the Greek penal code, which is consistent with the provisions of United States Foreign Corrupt Practices Act (FCPA) and the United Kingdom Bribery Act (UKBA)." The requirements of the UN Convention against Corruption are reflected in the enacting legislation which has been issued by its signatory states (including Greece). As a result, given that MOTOR OIL Group's Policy provisions are consistent with the Greek Penal Code which has incorporated the provisions of the UN Convention against corruption, MOTOR OIL Group's Policy is, in compliance with the Convention.

MOTOR OIL Group does not adhere to a specific animal welfare policy, as it is not applicable to its operations. The Group remains committed to following all relevant legal and ethical standards in its industry. Business Conduct Policies apply to all employees and management teams of the Group's companies, as well as to any parties representing any company of the Group or working on their behalf.

Additionally, the Group Code of Ethics and Corporate Responsibility ("the Code") establishes a robust framework for ethical conduct, transparency and integrity. The Group recognizes that corruption and bribery pose serious risks to business sustainability, regulatory compliance and stakeholder trust. Therefore, the Code sets out strict policies and actions aimed at preventing, detecting and addressing corruption and bribery across all levels of operation.

The Group Whistleblowing Policy also serves as a mechanism to prevent and detect any wrongdoings including corruption and bribery. In the anti-bribery and anti-corruption process, there are specific steps that should be followed for investigating potential breaches or concerns.

Upon receiving information or allegations of an incident, the Compliance Unit promptly records it, conducts an initial risk assessment and acknowledges written reports within three business days. Each case is evaluated for credibility, scale and severity to determine the appropriate response.

High-risk incidents are escalated to Top Management and the Head of Legal to assess potential exposure under bribery laws. With management's approval, the Compliance Unit collaborates with Internal Audit to investigate whether company controls failed, policies were violated, or disciplinary actions are required. Investigations may involve interviews, document reviews and external experts when necessary.

Findings are documented, corrective actions implemented, and the register updated. If appropriate, the original reporter is informed of the outcome. To maintain objectivity and independence, the investigation of corruption or bribery allegations is conducted by a dedicated internal investigative committee or external experts who are separate from the management teams responsible for implementing anti-corruption measures.

Upon conclusion, investigative results are formally reported to the relevant governing bodies, detailing findings, corrective measures and recommendations to enhance anti-corruption controls. The Compliance Unit ensures proper follow-up, including disciplinary actions, policy updates and training enhancements.

Within the process of the whistleblowing, the Reporting Management Committee (RMC) is responsible for managing and investigating reports within the organization, consisting of three key members: the Officer Responsible for the Receipt and Monitoring of Reports (ORRMR) or, if no ORRMR is appointed, the Group Compliance Officer, the Chief Human Resources Officer and the Director of Internal Audit. Depending on the results of the investigation, the ORRMR shall submit the investigation report to the Board of Directors and after consultation with the other members of the RMC, propose corrective actions to be taken. The final decision on the recommendations of the ORRMR shall be taken by the Board of Directors.

To strengthen internal controls, the Group has implemented monitoring mechanisms to detect and mitigate corruption risks. These mechanisms include regular audits, risk assessments and due diligence processes for third parties. Employees and business partners are required to disclose any potential conflicts of interest that may lead to unethical practices or improper advantages.

Training and awareness programs form a critical part of the Group's anti-corruption framework including the BoD. Employees receive ongoing education on recognizing and addressing bribery and corruption risks. The Group promotes a culture of ethical decision-making, ensuring that all personnel understand the severe consequences of engaging in corrupt activities, both legally and reputationally. The Compliance Unit, after the BoD approval, informs the Communications & Public Relations Division to upload at the Company's website the Policies, as foreseen by the legislative framework.

Moreover, the Compliance Unit uploads these corporate documents (including the relevant policy) at the Company's SharePoint. The training is conducted across the organization without taking into consideration the functions at risk. A key aspect of the training focuses on roles and responsibilities at different levels of the organization. Senior executives and the BoD receive specialized sessions emphasizing leadership in ethical decision-making and their duty to foster a corporate culture of compliance. Operational and functional teams receive targeted training tailored to their specific roles, ensuring that those involved in high-risk functions such as procurement, sales and finance understand the unique risks they may encounter.

Furthermore, the Group actively engages with regulatory authorities, industry associations and stakeholders to enhance anti-bribery measures and promote a corruption-free business environment. Cooperation with external entities strengthens compliance and reinforces the Group's commitment to ethical business conduct. The Code of Ethics and Corporate Responsibility is a dynamic document that evolves with regulatory changes and industry developments. The Board of Directors reserves the right to amend or update the Code upon recommendation from the Compliance Unit. Any modifications are communicated effectively to ensure continued adherence by all stakeholders.

During the reporting period, MOTOR OIL Group recorded zero convictions, fines, or confirmed incidents related to anti-corruption and anti-bribery laws. Additionally, there were no cases in which employees were dismissed or disciplined, nor were any contracts with business partners terminated due to corruption or bribery violations. Furthermore, there were no public legal cases filed against the Group or its employees in this regard.

### 11.4.3 Payment Practices [G1-6]

MOTOR OIL Group maintains structured and transparent payment practices across its business segments, ensuring clarity and consistency in supplier transactions. The Group's standard payment terms vary based on the nature of procurement and supplier category, with specific terms communicated to vendors through formal procurement documents. Furthermore, in specific cases, payment terms are established on a contractual basis, in addition to the standard payment practices.

In the Retail segment, the standard payment term is 90 days. Suppliers are informed of the payment conditions and processes through the RFQs sent to them, ensuring transparency and alignment with contractual agreements.

In the Circular Economy and Alternative Fuels segment, payments are typically processed under 25 days. The Group's approach in this segment is based on long-term relationships and market knowledge rather than a predefined methodology. Additionally, the company THALIS Environmental Services Single Member S.A., a technical firm within this segment, executes public works under contracts signed with the relevant public authorities. As a result, payments to subcontractors and suppliers are contingent on disbursements made by the respective public authority. Nevertheless, the company prioritizes payments to small and medium-sized enterprises, striving to fulfill its obligations to suppliers promptly and minimize delays. Supplier evaluation and selection are conducted based on technical specifications, which are received along with the tender documents accompanying each public procurement process.

Within the Renewable Energy Sources segment, the standard payment term is 30 days, with all RFx (Requests for Offer/Quotation/ Proposal) documents and contracts clearly communicating invoicing and payment milestones to vendors. For SMEs (Small - Medium enterprises) prepayment could be made upon request.

For Refining, Supply & Trading, payment terms are structured according to contractual terms. Based on the payment data from the group companies regarding payments to suppliers, the estimated average invoice settlement period is 29 days, with 53% of payments being made on time. It is important to note that, due to the diverse operations of several group companies, late payments do not constitute a breach of the agreed payment terms but rather reflect mutually agreed arrangements between the relevant parties. Consequently, the aforementioned results should be considered as indicative. The number of outstanding legal proceedings for late payments for 2024 is four (4).

# Entity Specific [ES]

## Strategy

### 12.1 Strategy

#### 12.1.1 Material Impacts, Risks and Opportunities

MOTOR OIL Group actively drives innovation and digital transformation as key enablers of efficiency, performance improvement and long-term value creation. These initiatives aim to minimize negative impacts while amplifying positive contributions across economic, environmental and social dimensions. By integrating cutting-edge technologies and IT-enabled capabilities, the Group enhances operational effectiveness, streamlines processes and strengthens its competitive position in the evolving energy landscape. Additionally, the introduction of innovative products and services supports market growth and aligns with global sustainability trends.

A core material impact for MOTOR OIL Group is technological advancement and digitalization, which reinforce business agility and resilience. The IT Division, closely aligned with the Group's overall strategy, leads the implementation of state-of-the-art systems that optimize decision-making, enhance customer value propositions and facilitate the transition to a new energy era. These efforts contribute not only to operational efficiency but also to sustainability by enabling data-driven energy management, emissions reduction and improved resource utilization.

Further to that, security and emergency preparedness remain paramount for the Group, given the potential risks inherent in the oil and gas industry. Large-scale incidents could have severe consequences for human safety, the environment and business continuity. To mitigate these risks, the Group maintains rigorous security protocols, implements proactive risk management measures and ensures compliance with all relevant national and European regulations, including the Seveso-III-Directive (2012/18/EU). These efforts safeguard employees, local communities and corporate assets while maintaining the integrity of operations (more information provided in next paragraphs).

#### 12.2 Impact, risk and opportunity management

##### 12.2.1 Innovation and Digital Transformation | Policies, Actions and Targets

MOTOR OIL Group continues to drive technological advancement by strategically investing in cuttingedge IT systems that align with the Group's broader vision. The IT Division plays a pivotal role in this transformation, enabling business-driven technology initiatives that support the transition to a new energy era while enhancing the customer value proposition.

A testament to this commitment, MOTOR OIL's IT General Division - IT Success Stories showcases key projects, benchmarks and strategic initiatives, underscoring the Group's focus on continuous improvement and operational excellence. In 2024, the IT budget was allocated to a range of initiatives, with a significant percentage dedicated to innovation-driven projects that contribute to long-term sustainability and efficiency.

Reflecting the Group's emphasis on Environmental, Social and Governance (ESG) priorities, several projects undertaken in 2024 highlight the role of digital transformation in optimizing processes, strengthening governance and reinforcing sustainability-driven business strategies.

##### Lab - Samples management for packaging products

With this project it is successfully monitored the sampling of packaged products, printing barcode labels for samples, recording chemistry analysis results and implementing corrective actions. A mobile application is utilized to support field usage decisions, with the added functionality of photo capture for documentation purposes.

##### Hydrogen Modelling & Green certification

Green hydrogen comprises a strategic domain for MOTOR OIL Group. Strategy department of MOTOR OIL and HELLENIC HYDROGEN have worked together to contribute to techno-economical modelling, define automated tools required for enhanced evaluations and well as contribute to the certification requirements anticipated for green hydrogen production.

##### CRACOWI Project

CRACoWi is a European project that seeks to meet the escalating cybersecurity challenges in the European digital market. Cyber Resilience Act - Compliance - Wizard" is being developed by the CRACoWi consortium to support SME manufacturers, distributors and importers in meeting the cybersecurity prerequisites throughout the product supply chain. Wizard emphasizes cybersecurity from a product's inception through its lifecycle, offering automated CRA compliance assessments, documentation generation and enhanced product security. MOTOR OIL Group's IT Division created a real-world use case for the project by leveraging MORE's assets, providing access to its own substation for a pilot trial.

##### SPARCS Project

It successfully completed a 5-year European funded project named "Sustainable energy Positive & zero cARbon Communities". The overall goal of SPARCS was to demonstrate and validate innovative solutions for planning, deploying and rolling out smart and integrated energy systems that transform cities into sustainable, citizen-centered, zero carbon ecosystems that offer a high quality of life for urban residents.

##### RISE Project

<!-- Report Page 276 -->
Project RISE, initiated by the Energy Solutions and Strategic Projects division of MOTOR OIL in collaboration with CORAL, AVIN, CI and BFS, aims to assess the profitability of a hybrid PV, battery storage and EV charging solution at refueling stations. The project explores large-scale roll-out potential, focusing on energy production, storage and flexibility. Its goal is to optimize consumption, reduce energy costs, minimize environmental impact and improve facility efficiency using PV-generated energy. Additionally, new revenue streams have been identified through innovative energy solutions like dynamic tariffs and demand response services.

##### Establishing the AI Center of Excellence - AI Center Of Excellence

Group is focused on building a dedicated internal AI team to explore and harness the potential of artificial intelligence across various areas within NRG. The team will identify and implement AI opportunities to drive innovation and enhance operational efficiency. Key initiatives include:

· Preventive Maintenance

· Autonomous Stores. Dynamic Pricing

· Stores Network Planning. People Count / In-Store Journeys

· Al in Salesforce Marketing. Energy Generation Forecasting. HR SPOT

This AI-driven approach will ensure that NRG stays at the forefront of innovation, continuously improving its processes and customer offerings.

##### Energy Low Voltage Consumption Forecasting

By enhancing NRG's competitive edge with a robust tool, it reduces forecasting errors in consumption and optimize resource planning. This AI-driven forecasting system enables us to achieve a level of precision in predicting energy demand. It is a powerful solution with the potential to strengthen its market position and elevate its decision-making capabilities in an increasingly competitive landscape.

##### AVIN Voice of Customer

It collects real-time customer feedback through a variety of channels, including QR codes, SMS, in-app surveys and station-specific surveys tailored to meet the unique needs of each location. Feedback is captured from both Loyalty and non-Loyalty members, providing a comprehensive view of customer sentiments. Utilizing advanced analytics and live reporting, it helps, to monitor and analyze this feedback in real time, enabling informed decision-making and continuous improvement in the customer experience.

##### Food Waste Management

To address food waste across CORAL, AVIN and CORE INNOVATIONS retail sites, Group is implementing a multi-faceted approach aimed at reducing waste and enhancing sustainability efforts. A key strategy involves offering expiring products at discounted prices to encourage consumption before expiration. Additionally, it will leverage geolocation technologies to target customers effectively, ensuring they are informed of nearby discount offers. Through loyalty app, customers will receive timely notifications regarding discounted products, further incentivizing purchases. This initiative also aims to gather valuable sustainability insights, enabling the entity to continuously improve its waste reduction practices and drive more sustainable business operations.

##### 12.2.2 Emergency Preparedness | Policies, Actions and Targets

Security and emergency preparedness are fundamental priorities for MOTOR OIL Hellas, given the inherent risks associated with the oil and gas sector. Large-scale incidents can have significant consequences for people, the environment and operations, while effective emergency response presents an opportunity to mitigate risks, ensure business continuity and safeguard employees, local communities and financial stability.

MOTOR OIL Group maintains a state of readiness across all functions, integrating stringent security measures and response protocols that align with national and European regulations. Compliance with the Seveso-III-Directive (2012/18/EU) underscores the company's commitment to accident prevention and impact mitigation, ensuring that even in the event of an incident, consequences on human health and the environment are minimized.

A clear demonstration of MOTOR OIL Group preparedness was evident in 2024 when a fire erupted in the southern section of MOTOR OIL (HELLAS) CORINTH REFINERIES S.A. in Agioi Theodoroi. Thanks to the prompt and coordinated efforts of both the Fire Department and refinery personnel, the fire was swiftly contained, preventing any threat to the surrounding area. During the fire incident three (3) members of the staff of a subcontracting company sustained minor injuries.

While the incident resulted in property damage, the Company was fully insured both with regards to property damages and the loss of operational profits due to the interruption of its operations. No danger exists for the Refinery area or the area surrounding it because of the fire incident.

MOTOR OIL Group continuously strengthens its security and emergency response framework, recognizing that preparedness is not just a regulatory requirement but a strategic imperative in protecting its workforce, communities and corporate reputation.

The requirements include:. Notification of all concerned establishments

<!-- Report Page 278 -->
· Deploying a major accident prevention policy

· Producing a safety report for upper tier establishments

· Producing internal emergency plans for upper tier establishments

· Providing information in case of accidents

###### Emergency response plans

All MOTOR OIL Group's facilities have made emergency response arrangements based on the Emergency Response Plan (ERP). The ERP is regularly reviewed and updated to consider any changing legal requirements and revised "best practices", resulting from technical advancements, gained experience or from peers in dealing with minor or major accidents.

Moreover, the Group prepares and submits appropriate studies to the relevant authorities, in accordance with the EU Seveso III Directive, covering the most likely accident scenarios, as well as relative prevention and response measures.

The Emergency Response Plans outline public health protection measures, including warnings and relevant advice for neighboring communities, to be implemented if the plans are activated due to a major technological accident. These plans are drafted by the competent authorities and determine how the State might intervene in emergency situations. MOTOR OIL Group maintains an emergency response system at its facilities, which includes studies, procedures and different response plans depending on the situation (fire, earthquake, terrorist act, flood, etc.). It has also organized emergency teams (fire brigades, evacuation teams, etc.), training programs and preparedness exercises. Finally, it has a set of plans and processes (e.g., internal emergency plans, fuel shortage plan, Group emergency corporate plan, BCP, etc.) for all possible emergency situations.

MOTOR OIL has been strategically prepared to address the scenarios outlined in its emergency plans. Emphasizing the importance of continuous proactivity and preparedness, the Group has also implemented a Corporate Contingency Plan. The Corporate Contingency Plan is designed to assess the capabilities of each company within the group, encompassing both human resources and infrastructure. It further aims to identify the most effective ways to align these capabilities, ensuring that, in the shortest possible time, they can seamlessly integrate and contribute to addressing emergency situations that may threaten the operations of any company within the group.

For further protection of the interests, assets and functions of the Group, contracts include three types of insurance risks: cessation of activity, accident & gradual pollution, against third parties.

CORAL GAS carries out a thorough risk management assessment, which is performed on an annual basis at management team level and has as its scope the identification of potential risks, the evaluation of responses and the provision of detailed control actions.

To assess the risks associated with OFC's operations, in order, to better manage them, several preparedness exercises are performed, such as the large-scale exercise that takes place annually, in which OFC participates jointly with the Athens International Airport (AIA) and a periodic study on the Emergency Response Plan (ERP).

<!-- Report Page 280 -->
##### Security Operations management

To demonstrate and improve security in operations, MOTOR OIL designed a framework (certified in accordance with ISO 18788) for establishing, implementing, operating, monitoring, reviewing, maintaining and improving a Security Operations Management System. The framework aims to safeguard the respect for human rights, the national and international laws and the fundamental freedoms by meeting the Guiding Principles on Business and Human Rights of the United Nations "Protect, Respect and Remedy" framework policy 2011.

##### Privacy Protection

MOTOR OIL Group follows GDPR standards and thus, has the duty to safeguard the data of its staff and customers. The Group understands that data privacy is essential on building the confidence of its customers and expanding its business. Therefore, the Group has made privacy protection a key strategic goal for us, and it has implemented a thorough data protection management program, robust governance processes to safeguard the privacy and security of customer information and to adhere to privacy laws. Through Group's data protection management program, it has designated a Data Protection Officer and has created corporate policies and procedures governing the collection, use, retention and protection of data.

Data protection and Group's prioritized data privacy, achieved by involving experts in the design phase of projects involving personal data, developing and implementing safeguards against data breaches. The Group maintains comprehensive records of data processing activities. Has procedures for monitoring, identifying and responding to any event that can lead to a data breach. Have procedures in place for notifying authorities and affected individuals if needed.

Further, the Group conducts Data Protection Impact Assessments for high-risk processing activities. Furthermore, signs Data Privacy Agreements with all its data processors and defines data retention periods. It ensures compliance with Data Privacy Agreements, minimize data processed for analytics, define retention periods and prepare systems to delete personal data once the retention period ends.

MOTOR OIL Group provides GDPR training to its employees. A mandatory Group-wide GDPR training and awareness workshops, previously provided to its employees, covered all GDPR requirements, including, notably, the reporting and management of personal data breaches.

##### Cybersecurity

With the increase of connected devices, systems, platforms and services MOTOR OIL Group understands the need for a strong emphasis on cybersecurity to safeguard the opportunities created by this interlinked, data-driven energy model and comply with NIS2 Regulation and other EU Cybersecurity Directives and Regulations. Based on the above the Group has designated a Chief Information Security Officer who is referred to the Deputy CEO.

MOTOR OIL Group has a 3-year planned, approved and deliberate strategy to deal with cybersecurity risk. The safeguarding of the Group information and operation assets' confidentiality, integrity and availability as well as making dedicated investments to incorporate cybersecurity into every element of Group's business model to ensure resiliency is vital to the business.

MOTOR OIL Group has set up a comprehensive and consistent Information Security Management System (ISMS) to identify and apply a complete range of information and operation security controls within the overall structure of a coherent management system. Group's comprehensive approach to cybersecurity includes the establishment of specific procedures and the application of organizational and technical measures that are continuously checked, monitored, enhanced and operates within the scope of the Group's activities. To achieve specific security and business objectives, MOTOR OIL Group determines, implements, monitors, evaluates and improves these controls where necessary. As a result, MOTOR OIL's headquarters and refinery has achieved ISO/IEC 27001:2023 Certification.

To ensure that the Information Security Management System works effectively and improves over time, the Group developed a consistent method for measuring and evaluating its performance. This method applies to the Information Security Management System of MOTOR OIL Group. Every year, the Group evaluates and updates its security performance standards and compliance level and uses clear metrics to measure them, such as Key Performance Indicators (KPIs), Key Risk Indicators (KRIs) and Key Goals Indicators (KGIs).

MOTOR OIL Group employees are a key component in its first line of defense. So, the Group always ensure that all relevant personnel are aware of the criticality and importance of their information security responsibilities and how they can contribute to the achievement of the ISMS' objectives. To do this, the Group has a mature security awareness and education program, in which all employees are required to complete privacy and information security training on an ongoing basis, including privacy compliance, information lifecycle governance, managing information and data, creating a safe cyber environment and business continuity. MOTOR OIL Group's awareness and education program includes, Information security training modules (videos, newsletters), targeted security awareness training for high-risk audiences, monthly simulated phishing exercises and security awareness communications.

##### Business Continuity

MOTOR OIL Group has designed the Business Continuity Management System (BCMS) that develops business continuity appropriate to the amount and type of impact that the Group may or may not accept following a disruption.

The purpose of a BCMS is to prepare for, provide and maintain controls and capabilities for managing the Group's overall ability to continue to operate during disruptions. The Business Continuity Management System (BCMS) enhances the overall efficiency of Group's organization by enabling it to allocate the appropriate financial and human resources, ensuring the continued operation of the Group. Business Continuity Plan (BCP) is a system of prevention and recovery from potential threats and risks including cyber-attacks, pandemics, natural disasters, human errors. BCP ensures that personnel and assets are protected and are able to function quickly in the event of an emergency. The BCMS Group's has developed, meets the requirements set forth by ISO 22301 for its implementation, maintenance and continuous improvement. It also ensures alignment with the established business continuity policy, enabling the continued delivery of products and services at an acceptable predefined capacity during disruptions, while enhancing organizational resilience through the effective application of the BCMS.

During the development of BCMS, MOTOR OIL Group has,. identified critical business services and units of MOTOR OIL's HQ and Refinery, critical records, service providers per business unit, as well as the dependencies per business unit.. executed a Business Impact Analysis for all businesses processes.. established and documented a Business Continuity Policy as well as a set of policies, which are aligned to the business's strategic direction and reflect that the BCMS is supported and adequately resourced.. documented emergency plans to effectively address all emergencies identified. Emergency plans are accessible and clearly communicated to all personnel.

· assigned the responsibility for emergency preparedness to different teams. Teams are responsible for planning, implementation and development of the Business Continuity Plan (BCP), for monitoring the established alert levels.

In conclusion, a robust Business Continuity framework is essential for ensuring the resilience and sustained operations of the MOTOR OIL Group, even in the face of unforeseen disruptions.
